#2181. Watford hold talks with 48-year-old boss who has been out of work since October 2025

Watford are sat 20th in the Championship table with 15 points from as many games. The Hornets have struggled for any sort of momentum, putting pressure on manager Alessio Dionisi despite the club hierarchy’s reported desire to give the boss more time than some of his predecessors.

Now though, a report from Romanian journalist Emanuel Rose states Watford have held talks with Edward Iordanescu over the managerial position at Vicarage Road. Contact has been described as ‘positive’, but there have not been any concrete English reports that the Championship side are considering a managerial change.

Iordanescu is available having been out of the game since leaving Legia Warsaw in October 2025. He previously managed Romania’s national team, alongside prestigious clubs such as FCSB and CFR Cluj.

Iordanescu is a 48-year-old coach who started his career in the dugout in 2010 after retiring in 2004. He was only 32 when he was first named as interim manager of Steaua Bucharest, where he stayed as an assistant coach to Ilie Stan until 2012.

He spent short spells in charge of Fortuna Brazi and FCM Targu Mures in Romania before first earning acclaim at Pandurii Targu Jiu, where his work earned him the CSKA Sofia job in Bulgaria. That stint lasted only 12 games though, and Iordanescu would return to his native in spells at Astra Giurgiu, CFR Cluj (x2), Gaz Metan and FCSB.

The rumoured Watford target managed the Romania national side from January 2022 to July 2024, winning 10 of 28 games and leading them to the UEFA Euro 2024 round of 16 before leaving at the end of the tournament.

A return to club football followed at Legia Warsaw in Poland, but they struggled in European and domestic football with Iordanescu criticised for inconsistent team selection, fruitless tactical changes, poor physical output metrics and disenchantment within the squad.

Iordanescu won the Romanian Superliga once and their Super Cup twice, and the same trophy in Poland once. His most used formation is a 4-3-3, but has also deployed his sides in a 4-2-3-1 setup.

As touched on earlier, nothing has been concretely reported by English or local outlets that Watford are planning a change in the dugout. However, rumoured talks with Iordanescu could indicate that they are staying prepared if they do sack Dionisi at the very leasy.

If there is a time to change manager, it is now. The international break offers a chance to get a new boss in and settled before the return to league action.

Iordanescu could be brought in swiftly and given time on the training ground to work with his new squad before club football returns against Sheffield United on November 21st. However, it remains to be seen if anything comes of the links, with managerial speculation never far around the corner at Vicarage Road.

#2179. Veronica Squinzi: "Mapei guarda al Sassuolo per vedere lontano e investire per il futuro"

Veronica Squinzi, amministratrice delegata del Sassuolo, è intervenuta direttamente dal Mapei Football Center in occasione del premio Golden Boy 2026, raccontando come funziona il centro sportivo dei neroverdi: "Qui puoi vedere giocare insieme i piccoli Pulcini e le prime squadre, che è una cosa anche molto particolare. È molto motivante per i ragazzi, perché - si legge su SassuoloNews - avere la possibilità di vedere i grandi campioni giocare e vedere che si può riuscire è sempre molto stimolante. In Mapei abbiamo sempre guardato al Sassuolo in un modo molto semplice, con la nostra visione, quella che utilizziamo tutti i giorni: vedere lontano, investire per il futuro, non essere focalizzati sul breve ma avere una visione di medio-lungo termine".

Un occhio viene buttato anche alla Prima Squadra: "Ovviamente è il volto più visibile di questo percorso, ma il nostro progetto coinvolge ogni area del club con l'obiettivo di creare un ambiente in cui le persone possano crescere sia come atleti, ma anche come persone, come individui. E quindi non può che esserci una grande attenzione verso i giovani. Un investimento che il club porta avanti sia nel settore maschile sia in quello femminile. Squinzi ha ricordato come il vivaio femminile neroverde sia oggi tra i più numerosi in Italia, con 11 squadre e 303 ragazze, mentre quello maschile conta 12 formazioni e oltre 253 giovani.

Ciò che conta davvero è il percorso di crescita che seguiamo ogni giorno. Ogni anno vediamo ragazze e ragazzi compiere passi in avanti non solo dal punto di vista tecnico ma anche da quello umano, imparando il valore dell'impegno, della disciplina, del rispetto delle regole, del rispetto delle persone e della collaborazione".

Sotto l’ultimo post ufficiale della Lazio, i tifosi chiedono al club di prolungare il contratto del centrocampista.

#2177. 3 players who Middlesbrough should offload in January, including Micah Hamilton

Middlesbrough sit 2nd in the Championship table with the latest international break upon us. It has been a fine campaign for Boro to date, but they won’t be in any mood to rest on their laurels on the pitch or in the transfer market.

Steve Gibson backed Kim Hellberg heavily in the summer with pricey deals for Will Lankshear, Radek Vitek and more. However, there will be more opportunities in the January transfer window, and incomings and outgoings are expected at the Riverside Stadium.

To streamline Hellberg’s squad and make way for new signings, we put forward three players who Middlesbrough should consider offloading in the winter…

Experienced centre-back George Edmundson was lined up for a move away from Middlesbrough late in the summer transfer window, but a deal never materialised. He has not been named in a Championship matchday squad since the opening-day win over Lincoln City and is firmly down the pecking order under Hellberg.

With his contract expiring at the end of the season, the 29-year-old seems bound to leave Boro at some point in 2027. It would be beneficial for him to move on sooner rather than later though, and a winter transfer will allow him to get back to playing.

The powerful centre-back could be a shrewd signing for a team towards the bottom end of the Championship, or in the top six of League One.

Capped by England at U18 and U20 levels, McCabe is among Middlesbrough’s brightest young prospects. However, his progression has stalled somewhat in 2026 off the back of a tough loan at Plymouth Argyle.

The 20-year-old has been back in the U21s setup this season with not one senior appearance made. McCabe turns 21 in January though, so he is at the age where he needs to get more first-team football under his belt.

Given his promise, it seems unlikely that he leaves Teesside on a permanent basis. However, the club must strongly consider sending the young midfielder out on loan to a team where he is a strong tactical fit and will get plenty of opportunities to start.

Last but not least is Micah Hamilton, who is sure to be angling for a chance to kick his career back into life. His two years at Middlesbrough have not brought the opportunities he would have hoped for, but he still has exciting potential to tap into after his emergence at hometown club Manchester City.

The 22-year-old’s contract at the Riverside runs until 2028. But, with the likes of Rokas Pukstas, Myles Peart-Harris, Jeremy Sarmiento and Amario Cozier-Duberry arriving in the attacking midfield and wing positions during the summer, it seems unlikely that Hamilton forces his way into Hellberg’s plans.

Perhaps an impressive loan would change that, but it may be best for all parties if a permanent exit deal is struck in January. Given his high ceiling, there will be clubs further down in the EFL ready to take a chance on Hamilton if the price is right.

#2176. Inter Women, Sassarini: "Sconfitta diversa da quella contro il Lione. Mancano 3 punti per qualificarci"

Inter Women, Sassarini: "Sconfitta diversa da quella contro il Lione. Mancano 3 punti per qualificarci"

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

David Sassarini, tecnico dell'Inter Women, ha commentato ai microfoni di Fcinter1908 la sconfitta rimediata contro il Manchester City in Champions League: "La verità è che stiamo giocando contro le squadre più forti al mondo. Ci sono grandi differenze tra la partita di Lione e questa: se andiamo a vedere il valore della squadra c'è differenza, e se guardiamo gli episodi non ci sono girati benissimo. A 30 secondi dalla fine del primo tempo prendiamo gol, e potevamo anche evitare: c'è rammarico, perchè se vai nello spogliatoio con il punteggio di 1-1 c'è un approccio diverso. Sul 2-1 abbiamo avuto un'occasione grande per fare il 2-2, e moralmente avrebbe spezzato la gara. Queste giocatrici hanno grande qualità, sanno giocare nello stretto, mettono palla tra le linee e vanno al cross con facilità. Noi ancora dobbiamo imparare a crescere, ma le occasioni a favore e contro probabilmente sono sullo stesso piano".

Il gol iniziale di Bartoli è stata l'espressione del piano di gioco iniziale.

"Per la prima volta abbiamo giocato dietro a 5, nel secondo tempo siamo tornati a giocare come d'abitudine e abbiamo creato parecchie situazioni. Ripartiamo, abbiamo delle qualità, poi è chiaro che stiamo giocando contro squadre che sono di un livello superiore. C'è bisogno di un percorso, per arrivare a quel livello in un anno cercando scorciatoie sarebbe anche ingeneroso in quello che sono i valori del calcio e del lavoro. Bisogna allenarsi, ma queste partite insegnano".

"Sappiamo che ci mancano 3 punti per qualificarci: abbiamo due partite per poterlo fare, ci proveremo in tutti i modi, già dalla prossima contro il Real Madrid, oppure con l'Arsenal, oppure in entrambe. Di sicuro l'obiettivo è quello di provare a qualificarci".

Tommaso Bianchini, direttore Generale dell’Area Business della SSC Napoli, ha partecipato al panel “Club, non solo Squadre”, nell’ambito dell’edizione 2026 del Social Football Summit, in corso all’Allianz Stadium di Torino. #2174. Mussa: "Rinnovo Calhanoglu? Ecco cosa fare se fossi nell'Inter. Hakan invece deve..."

#2173. Francia, litigio tra Mbappé e Dembelé: c'entra il Pallone d'Oro. Pesa l'assenza di Thuram

Il raduno della Francia è iniziato ufficialmente a Clairefontaine, con il commissario tecnico Zinedine Zidane costretto a gestire una serie di defezioni pesanti prima delle sfide di Nations League contro Italia e Turchia. Pierre Kalulu, Marcus Thuram e Aurélien Tchouameni sono finiti ko, ma l'attenzione del nuovo selezionatore è rivolta alla gestione di Kylian Mbappé, atteso a un cambio tattico importante: vista l'assenza di Ousmane Dembélé, l'attaccante del Real Madrid dovrebbe infatti essere impiegato come prima punta centrale anziché sulla fascia sinistra, riprendendo l'esperimento interrotto a settembre dopo meno di un'ora a causa dell'infortunio al ginocchio patito in Turchia.

Sul piano dello spogliatoio, la posizione del capitano appare tuttavia più complessa e isolata rispetto al passato. Secondo quanto rivelato da L'Équipe, la campagna per il Pallone d'Oro ha incrinato i rapporti tra il madrileno e Dembélé, sfociati in un gelo evidente. L'esterno parigino non avrebbe infatti risposto a un messaggio WhatsApp inviatogli da KM10 per chiarire le divergenze, reazione giudicata sproporzionata dal capitano Bleus ma che ha lasciato strascichi evidenti all'interno del gruppo transalpino.

A complicare le cose per Mbappe si aggiunge la mancanza della sua abituale "guardia scelta" e il forfait del connazionale Marcus Thuram, figura chiave nel fare da ponte tra i diversi componenti della rosa. I tentativi di Mbappé di riavvicinarsi a compagni e membri dello staff sono stati talvolta percepiti come insoliti o opportunistici da uno spogliatoio in continua evoluzione. Intanto, il classe '98 resta consapevole che la sua forza passerà innanzitutto attraverso l'impatto sul terreno di gioco, dove punta a trascinare di nuovo i Bleus per ricompattarsi con il resto della squadra.

#2172. Two goals, two assists: Derby County starlet staking claim for future first-team role as loan form continues

Derby County saw Owen Eames break into their first-team picture last term. The youngster has made six appearances for the DCFC senior side after impressive academy displays brought him onto John Eustace‘s radar.

But, the call was made to send him out on loan for the 2026/27 campaign to find more game time. Scottish Premiership side Falkirk snapped Eames up, and he impressed early on with a goal against Hearts and drew high praise from boss John McGlynn.

Over the weekend, Falkirk played Hearts for the second time this season, and Eames was on the scoresheet once again. Playing on their left wing, the Derby County loanee is earning rave reviews and conversations over his next steps are bound to follow.

Eames netted against Hearts in August and followed that up with an assist in the 1-1 draw against St. Johnstone in the match after. On November 1st, he set up Scott Tanser for the second goal in a 2-0 triumph over league leaders Celtic.

And, in his next league appearance last Sunday, Eames netted the Bairns’ second in a rampant win over Hearts. Falkirk countered after defending a corner and the Derby County loanee showed pace to offer a passing option, before duly side-footing home.

Eames was a constant handful, getting three shots off and completing three dribbles. In one instance, he pulled off a slick one-footed drag-back past one man and rouletted around the other while being grappled to cut inside and get a shot off on goal.

Owen Eames' dribbling 😮‍💨 pic.twitter.com/CrlDMWKaKt

Eames now has four goal contributions in nine games for Falkirk – two goals, and two assists. He has started every Scottish Premiership game since his arrival, locking down a starting spot and helping their five-match winning run to move up to 3rd in the table.

Eames’ first loan away from DCFC couldn’t be going much better, and it will be hoped he can continue in this vein.

Eames’ form has raised questions over the decision to loan him out in the first place, and some are wondering whether there is an option to bring him back to Derby County in January. It has not been publicly reported whether they have a recall clause at present.

At the moment though, it feels as though the youngster is in the perfect place for his development. He’s playing well and getting plenty of minutes for McGlynn’s Falkirk, and it will be doing wonders for his progress.

On his current trajectory, Eames seems bound to be considered for a place in the first-team squad in 2027. It just remains to be seen whether DCFC recall him if they can, or if the exciting prospect has to wait until next season for his Pride Park breakout opportunity.

#2170. 17 goals in 14 games: Middlesbrough flop and ex-Leicester City man hits stunning form with new club

Leicester City had Kelechi Iheanacho on their books from 2017 to 2024, bringing him in from Manchester City after his eye-catching break into senior football. The Nigerian striker was a mainstay for the Foxes, playing 232 times for the club across all competitions.

He struggled for consistent form for the most part, but did net 19 goals and provide seven assists in 39 games in a prolific 2020/21 campaign. Iheanacho was unable to rediscover that level, and after his departure from the King Power Stadium, he endured a tough half-season loan at Middlesbrough in 2022. He flopped as he managed one goal in 15 games.

A stint at Celtic in the 2025/26 season followed, before Iheanacho moved to Turkey with second tier side Bursaspor in the summer. And, the former Leicester City and Middlesbrough man has hit stunning form there.

Albeit against a lower level of opposition, Iheanacho is in the best form of his life with Bursaspor. The 30-year-old has scored a remarkable 17 goals in 14 league games this season.

The 59-cap Nigeria international went on a run of scoring in 12 consecutive league games, which included four braces. He fired a blank in a 3-0 win over Bandirma Sport at the end of October but followed that up with a return to scoring ways against Antalyaspor last week.

Iheanacho’s success comes from Bursaspor’s brilliant chance creation. He has taken 56 shots in total, equating to an average of four a game (FotMob). As such, with his finishing and experience, it is little wonder that the striker is enjoying such a goalscoring resurgence after his spells at Leicester City and Middlesbrough.

Bursaspor’s goal is to return to the Turkish top flight for the first time since 2019. They have rebuilt over the last two years after dropping all the way to the third tier and having a player of Iheanacho’s pedigree on a three-year deal displays their ambition.

As Iheanacho rediscovers his scoring touch at Bursaspor, Middlesbrough and Leicester City have found new star strikers of their own this season.

At Boro, Will Lankshear has been one of the Championship’s signings of the season. He has 15 goals in 16 games across all competitions, drawing interest from Newcastle United and catching the eye of England manager Thomas Tuchel.

Meanwhile at Leicester City, Emil Riis has hit form after joining for around £2m in the summer. The Foxes’ form has been inconsistent, but the Danish striker has eight goals in 11 games for the club.

#2169. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2168. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2167. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2166. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2165. EFL club eyeing ambitious stadium move

Stevenage knocked on the door of League One promotion last season, reaching the play-off semi-finals, and now the club are wanting to continue on their upward trajectory off the pitch, as well as on it with an ambitious stadium move.

Stevenage Borough, as they were then known, established itself as a football club in 1976 following the bankruptcy of Stevenage Athletic and the club, now in its 50th year, appears to continue to be going from strength to strength.

The phoenix club has risen from tenth-tier in the English football pyramid all the way to the third-tier, first entering into the Football League in the 2010-11 campaign – and they have not been out of it since.

In the 2011-12 season, following back-to-back promotions, Stevenage finished sixth in League One, which had been their best ever finish until equalling that last season ahead of a defeat to Stockport County in the play-off semi-finals.

With the club continuing to overperform on the pitch through impressive decision-making and general running of the club, ‘Boro are now seeking to take steps off the pitch to sustain themselves at their current level and beyond.

When Stevenage Borough, now just Stevenage, was formed 50 years ago, the club was forced to play at the town’s King George V playing fields due to a dispute before returning to previous home, Broadhall Way, in 1980.

Broadhall Way, which has gone on to be called the Lamex Stadium and now the SPORTA Arena, has remained their home ever since but, in a club statement released on Tuesday, chairman Phil Wallace has outlined ambitions to move on with the hope of increased income to sustain their competitiveness.

Wallace says: “For all the progress we have made, however, there is one fundamental part of the club that has not kept pace with our growth; our stadium.

"We are still operating on the same footprint that was provided 50 years ago for a non-league football club with 500 fans. Today, we are regularly attracting crowds ten times that size, with ambitions to go considerably further. Over the years, we have invested substantially in improving our home, increasing capacity to approximately 7,800. However, even with further investment to replace and expand the East and West Stands, the physical limitations of the site would restrict capacity to around 10,000 and the footprint would be unchanged. The reality is that our current stadium footprint is increasingly a constraint on our future.

"If we genuinely want to compete consistently at the upper end of League One and give ourselves the opportunity to establish Stevenage FC in the Championship, we must think differently.

"Over the Summer, we opened discussions with stakeholders to explore potential opportunities and sites for a new stadium-led development that becomes a significant community and commercial asset for Stevenage, bringing together sport, leisure, hospitality, retail, business and more, creating a destination that generates employment, attracts investment, and delivers well beyond matchdays. That search is now well under way.

"Other clubs are already recognising the opportunities presented by new stadium-led regeneration; Luton Town has commenced a new stadium development, while Cambridge United has recently outlined its own long-term ambitions for a similar project. Stevenage cannot be left behind and there are important questions to answer around location, funding, infrastructure and the wider needs of the town. However, I firmly believe both the ambition and the timing are right.

"We have always been a challenger club, taking on teams with greater resources and proving what can be achieved through hard work, smart investment and determination. Our next chapter should build on those foundations.

"But none of this means anything without our supporters. We want to compete at the highest level we can and to achieve that, we need the whole town behind us.

"We have achieved an enormous amount in our first 50 years. Let’s make the next 50 years even greater.”

Those clubs mentioned in the same local and regional area as Stevenage, Luton Town and Cambridge United, are themselves looking to expand.

Luton are further along in the process with The Hatters due to move out of their famous home, Kenilworth Road, and to the Power Court site in central Luton for the 2028-29 campaign. The new stadium is set to hold 25,000 as opposed to Kenilworth Road’s capacity of 12,056.

As for Cambridge, The U’s are exploring plans for a 15,000-capacity stadium in the east of the city as they seek to move away from the 7,937-capacity Abbey Stadium, which has been their home since 1932.

Stevenage have more or less been on an upward trajectory since their reformation in the 1970s and then kicking on in the 80s, with the club suffering just two relegations in their history; from Isthmian Division One in the 1987-88 campaign and then from League One in the 2013-14 season.

Having finished ninth, 14th and then sixth since returning to the third-tier in 2023; it is therefore now not unreasonable to suggest Stevenage will eventually likely batter down the door and get themselves into the second-tier, which would be an overachievement for many neutrals but one that similar sized clubs such as Lincoln City and Wycombe Wanderers have managed in the recent past.

The impressive off-field decision-making that has allowed for Stevenage to remain competitive is something Wallace also mentioned in his statement.

We rank all 24 League One stadiums by their ground's distance from the city or town, starting with the longest distance.

He said: “As the club celebrates its 50th anniversary, I believe this is the right moment to reflect on how far we have come and set out our ambitions for where we go next.

"We cannot predict what football will look like in 50 years. But we can, and absolutely should, plan for the next 10, 20 years and beyond.

"We have never been a club that stands still. Our philosophy has always been to build for the future, often ahead of where we are on the pitch. That approach has taken us from local non-league football to an established EFL club, competing nationally against clubs with significantly greater resources.

Over the past 15 years, we have transformed our football operation through sustained investment in our training grounds, stadium, Academy, and women’s team. More recently, we have expanded into new areas, including Rookies nursery and a new Padel facility, creating additional revenue streams that support the long-term future of the club.

"Pound for pound, I believe there are few clubs in English football that make better use of their resources than Stevenage FC, both on and off the pitch. Last season, we were the most efficient club in League One when measured by points achieved against playing budget, demonstrating what can be accomplished through intelligent investment and sound financial management.

"Our football recruitment strategy is a further example of that approach. We have built a model focused on identifying the right characters and talent, recognising untapped value. So far in 2026, we have generated more than £2.25 million in net player revenue while continuing to rebuild a competitive first-team squad and staff structure.”

An example would be that in the summer transfer window, manager Alex Revell had to deal with the sales of a couple of key players such as Filip Marschall and Jamie Reid, while Charlie Goode and Harvey White also departed.

Despite that, Stevenage continue to compete with their 1-0 defeat of Oxford United at the Kassam Stadium in the FA Cup first round at the weekend backing up a 2-2 draw with Leicester City away from home, leaving them six points off the play-off places with a steady but improving record of four wins, four draws and four defeats in their 12 League One matches thus far.

Despite the club remaining in a competitive spot on the pitch and clearly impressive work has been done to sustain their rise off the pitch, there has been a mixed response to the update from chairman Wallace.

Both Stevenage supporters and fans of other clubs, such as Huddersfield Town and Plymouth Argyle, have proffered the point that Stevenage don’t tend to sell out their current stadium and so a bigger one would surely just have the same issue and is therefore unnecessary.

Others, though, have acknowledged that the ambition of the club is exciting and the intentions of stadium regeneration is a further example of that.

Division and debate is a part of football and Stevenage may have to navigate some dissenters but Wallace and the club have made clear their plans to continue to push ‘Boro onto the next level.

#2164. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2163. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2162. Philippe Clement thinks Norwich City man can follow in Sunderland star’s footsteps

Norwich City boss Philippe Clement has compared Lucien Mahovo to former PSG and Borussia Dortmund player Thomas Meunier, now a Sunderland star, as the 21-year-old has been deployed in a more attacking role than expected since returning from injury.

Mahovo's emergence into the Norwich fold since he arrived from Notts County back in 2024 has been stunted by various setbacks, most recently an ankle injury during pre-season, which ruled him out for the first couple of months of the campaign.

Since returning to action, though, the 21-year-old has been substituted on as a right-sided attacker in league action, much to the surprise of Canaries fans, as he'd broken through as an attacking left-back.

Clement is honing in on his qualities going forward, while the likes of Ben Chrisene and Darlin Yongwa both battle for the starting left-back spot, and the Norwich boss feels he could thrive in this new role, just like a young Meunier did while he was on the coaching staff at Club Brugge.

It's safe to say that Philippe Clement rates Mahovo highly; he wouldn't be looking for ways to include him while the left-back spot remains pretty saturated otherwise.

Speaking to the press following their recent 5-0 demolition at Bristol City, he explained that, back in Bruges, a young Thomas Meunier was breaking through as an attacker before finding his feet further back.

The 35-year-old, off the back of that, has 83 Belgian caps and many years at the top of the game in Europe with Borussia Dortmund and PSG, before joining Sunderland this past summer and making his bow in the Premier League.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

"I spoke with Lucien about the plan in training sessions to put him higher, because I've seen good things with him in four-against-four, five-against-five, six-against-six in the small-sided games about dribbling, shooting, making decisive actions, and like this he has more chance to be in the selection and to come on the pitch, and that's his ambition," Clement said.

"Will it be his position for the rest of his life? I don't know, to be honest. Sometimes those things can happen, and suddenly you see something that changes a career.

"We had Thomas Meunier in Bruges, when I was assistant, as a striker. He became a winger with the manager there, and he did that job defensively well. And later on, he became a full back, and he made an international career, going to the best teams in the world as a right full back.

"He would never have been a Paris (Saint-Germain) player for years as a striker or a right winger, but as a right full back, yes."

Meunier has a career to be proud of, and if Clement is tipping Mahovo to have a similar impact as the 35-year-old has had following a slight positional tweak, then that's high praise.

It's not like Norwich aren't deprived of wingers, either. If the 21-year-old isn't playing at left-back, he'll be competing with Andre Brooks for that right-wing role, whom the Canaries signed for £8 million from Sheffield United in the summer.

The Belgian boss is evidently a big fan, though, and his defensive nous as a winger could help craft a well-rounded player, which would likely put him ahead in the pecking order for the majority of the coaches he'd play under.

Do you think Lucien Mahovo's future lies on the right-wing? Let us know in the comments.

"He has so much potential, the boy, and I believe so hard in his potential,” Clement continued. “Also offensively, he does his defensive duty. So I really think he can, in both positions, make an amazing career.

"I believe really hard in Lucien’s quality, not only as a full back but also in those positions to hurt opponents with this playing style, so those are things also to develop.”

Norwich are starting to hit their stride this season, with four successive wins lifting them into the top half at the November international break. Having everyone fit and healthy will be key if the Canaries are to continue to overcome yet another slow start and challenge for the play-offs.

Mahovo has the support and belief from his head coach. Now, he needs to remain available for him to maintain that, something which he's struggled to do now for a couple of years.

In the 2024/25 campaign under Johannes Hoff Thorup, the then-teenage full-back started six consecutive league games between January and February, and looked to be breaking through, before injury curtailed his year.

FLW looks at which of the division's 24 clubs are the biggest and lowest spenders

Then, last season, he looked to be getting back into contention under Clement over the Christmas period, but more muscular issues saw him miss almost the entire second half of the term.

Coming off the bench in each of his last three is a good sign that he may be able to make a sustained run in the side and, if he can, he'll be able to nail down a more important role in this exciting Norwich squad.

Mahovo has the potential, both defensively and going forward, but only time will tell whether he can stay healthy enough to reach it.

#2161. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

#2158. Taty Castellanos to leave West Ham? Everything we know right now

West Ham have had a disappointing start to the Championship season, with Nuno Espirito Santo having already paid the price for his job.

Many expected the Hammers to not only return to the Premier League at the first attempt, but to win the title, so the fact they are outside the play-off places after 15 games is a concern.

Of course, there is still time to turn things around, so new boss Scott Parker won’t be panicking, even if he knows that they must improve quickly.

When it comes to underperforming players, there are several individuals at West Ham who are not hitting their best levels, with Taty Castellanos arguably the biggest culprit, having scored just once in 14 Championship outings.

That will have come as a major surprise, as the 28-year-old was one of the few positives for the Irons as they were relegated last season.

After joining from Lazio in January, Castellanos scored six times in 18 Premier League games, which is a very decent return.

Who is the Championship's most valuable starting XI?

Therefore, it was widely expected that he would take the second tier by storm, but, as outlined above, Castellanos hasn’t delivered.

It’s not just his poor form in front of goal either, as Castellanos’ work-rate and intensity made him such a popular figure, and that appears to have dropped off as well.

Despite that, it has been claimed that Castellanos could move on in January, with reports stating that River Plate have made the 28-year-old a top target going into the New Year.

The chance to return to his home country may appeal to the two-cap Argentina international, and obviously River Plate are a huge club that will hope to compete for honours domestically and on the continent.

It has been said that River Plate would look to sign Castellanos on an initial loan deal, which may include an option or obligation to buy, which is hardly ideal for the Hammers, although it remains to be seen how negotiations play out.

Right now, it’s fair to think that West Ham will regret not cashing in on Castellanos in the summer window, when his value was at its highest.

His form in the Premier League understandably put him on the radar of clubs in the top division, and it was revealed that Everton had an offer worth up to £45m turned down by the Londoners prior to the summer deadline.

At the time, you could see why West Ham took that stance. There was an expectation that the striker would be huge in their promotion push this season, so retaining him was an ambition statement by the hierarchy after also keeping the likes of Konstantinos Mavropanos.

In hindsight, however, it is clearly a mistake, as whether it’s River Plate or another club, it’s clear that West Ham aren’t going to get anywhere near £45m for the ex-Girona man.

Most fans would urge West Ham not to accept a loan deal, and it’s easy to see why. Even if they won’t get near £45m, Castellanos could still command a huge fee.

That could be put towards a few new signings in January, and Parker will no doubt have his own targets that he wants to bring to the club.

But, if Castellanos does want to move to River Plate, and they’re only offering an initial loan, it could put the Hammers in a tricky spot. Do they risk keeping an unhappy player?

How much is Taty Castellos worth? Let us know in the comments below.

Ultimately, there is a lot of football to be played between now and January, so things can change, and Parker must do all he can to get Castellanos back to his best, as he could be a major asset to the team if he gets near that level.

If he can’t, though, then a January move seems inevitable, and West Ham may have to make a compromise to ship him out if it’s for the good of the club.

#2156. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

“He spoke very strongly after the game against Atletico Madrid, which Man Utd lost. It was a very negative moment for the Red Devils. We know that Licha was very strong in his comments. He said that Michael Carrick was not backed enough during the summer transfer window.”

“Licha apologised to Man Utd as a club. He spoke to the directors, he spoke to Jason Wilcox, and he spoke to Michael Carrick, who was also involved in this conversation.”

Romano also revealed that United had imposed a financial penalty on the centre-back. However, the punishment does not appear to involve removing him from the squad.

“According to the information available tonight, Lisandro Martinez has been fined.”

He added that Martinez is expected to return to the squad as normal, with the fine considered sufficient punishment for the incident.

“The internal discussion also helped to resolve all the issues. For Man Utd, the case is closed.”

Romano also pointed towards Martinez’s contract, which expires next summer, with United holding an option to extend it.

“So, let’s see what is going to happen there. For sure, this is the kind of situation that will have an impact on the situation surrounding Lisandro, but we have to see what happens next in terms of future transfers and everything else.”

The Blues replaced Middlesbrough at the summit on goal difference after a disappointing week for the Teesside outfit, who followed up their midweek defeat at Wrexham with an underwhelming 0-0 draw at Watford.

Surprise package Stoke City are level on points with Birmingham and Boro after their impressive run continued with a 2-1 victory at Portsmouth, while Midlands duo Wolverhampton Wanderers and West Bromwich Albion are just a point behind the trio.

Swansea, Southampton and Queens Park Rangers complete the play-off places, with West Ham United dropping out of the top eight after their turbulent start to life under new head coach Scott Parker continued with a 2-1 defeat at Blackburn Rovers.

While the aforementioned nine teams appear to be the most likely automatic promotion contenders as things stand, it is difficult to ignore a resurgent Norwich City, who recorded their fourth consecutive win as they hammered Bristol City 5-0 to move within three points of the play-offs.

The international break will give many of the promotion hopefuls a chance to rest after a busy period, but recent developments at Crystal Palace may have Middlesbrough and Swansea feeling a little uneasy.

There are yet to be any managerial sackings in the Premier League this season, but that appears certain to change during the international break as Pierre Sage's future at Crystal Palace hangs in the balance.

Sage only took over at Selhurst Park in the summer when he replaced Oliver Glasner, but the Eagles have won just two of their opening 10 league games, and they currently sit bottom of the table, two points from safety.

It seems the Eagles have already drawn up a shortlist of potential replacements for Sage, with Middlesbrough's Kim Hellberg and Swansea's Vitor Matos said to be two of the names under consideration, along with Thomas Frank, Frank Lampard, Sean Dyche, Vitor Pereira, Tony Popovic, Jens Berthel Askou and Ole Werner.

Former Brentford and Tottenham Hotspur boss Frank is reportedly a leading contender to succeed Sage, which may offer some reassurance to Middlesbrough and Swansea, but news that Hellberg and Matos are attracting top flight interest will be music to the ears of their promotion rivals.

Given that Middlesbrough stole Hellberg from under Swansea's noses last November before the Welsh side turned to Matos, there is a certain irony to the fact that the pair are now both on Crystal Palace's radar.

While Hellberg inherited a strong foundation at the Riverside Stadium after the work of his predecessor, Rob Edwards, the Swede has taken Boro to another level over the past year, building one of the most dangerous attacking sides in the division, as well as implementing an attractive brand of football.

After an 11th-placed finish last season, the Swans have emerged as a surprise promotion contender this time around under Matos, and they led the Championship during the early weeks of the campaign before a recent run of four defeats in five games saw them drop down to sixth.

There is no doubt that Hellberg and Matos have been integral to the success of their respective clubs this season, and it would be a devastating blow for Middlesbrough and Swansea were either to lose their highly rated coaches, while simultaneously providing a huge boost to the likes of Birmingham, Wolves, West Brom and West Ham.

Given that Boro have assembled one of the strongest squads in the Championship after their summer spending spree, it seems unlikely that Hellberg would be tempted to make the move to Selhurst Park, but Matos could consider jumping ship if the Eagles come calling.

Of course, the January window is always difficult, so it’s not going to be a case of overhauling the squad. Instead, the focus will be on bringing in one or two quality players that can enhance the group.

Whilst fans will all have their own opinions, the consensus is that Wolves need attacking reinforcements, so a new winger and striker will be the priorities.

And, Femi Azeez would be a smart signing for Wolves to strengthen their options out wide, as the likes of Rodrigo Gomes and Mateus Mane have been inconsistent this season, with Bertrand Traore yet to make his league debut due to injury issues.

There is an emphasis on wide play from the Wolves chief, who wants his wingers to hug the touchline, stretch the play and get at their opponents.

That makes Azeez the ideal fit, and the prospect of the Nigerian international linking up with the likes of Fer Lopez and Raul Jimenez in the New Year is one that would worry rivals at this level.

Following on from that, as outlined above, the January window is about Wolves making a few tweaks that can help them win promotion, and there’s no doubt Azeez fits the bill.

This is a player that scored 11 goals and registered seven assists in 37 Championship games last season with the Lions, so he is proven in the second tier.

Again, that’s something that is crucial in January, as you need players to hit the ground running, and Azeez should be capable of doing that given his pedigree at this level.

#2146. Como vs Man Utd Preview: Team news, injuries, form and where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United travel to Italy on Wednesday, looking for their first win since September 10, when they face Como in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to build any momentum this season. Sunday’s 1-1 draw against Leeds extended their winless run to six matches in all competitions, leaving United with just seven points from their opening seven Premier League games.

The pressure on Carrick continues to grow. A win in Europe would offer some relief, but Como will not make life easy for Manchester United.

Manchester United began their Champions League campaign with a convincing 4-0 win over Sabah FK but lost 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in their next match. They have since drawn with Leeds, having also failed to beat Fulham and Tottenham in the Premier League.

Their last six matches across all competitions have produced three draws and three defeats. Michael Carrick is in pressure of his own and needs a response from his players, particularly with United sitting 13th in the Premier League and 13th in the Champions League standings.

Como have shown more promise. Cesc Fabregas’ side beat RB Leipzig 4-1 in Europe before losing 3-2 to Feyenoord. They returned to winning ways against Fiorentina, claiming a 3-1 victory that moved them up to fifth in Serie A, with 14 points from seven games.

Como have scored 11 goals and conceded eight across their last six matches in all competitions. United have struggled, with their only clean sheet of the season coming against Sabah FK.

United have several confirmed absentees ahead of the trip to Italy. Patrick Dorgu, Manuel Ugarte and Benjamin Sesko are ruled out, while Leny Yoro is suspended for the European fixture after picking a red against Atletico Madrid.

Lisandro Martinez is a major concern after coming off at half-time against Leeds with a thigh problem. Ayden Heaven, Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are all doubtful for the game.

Como will be without Assane Diao, who is sidelined with a thigh injury. Moise Kean has a shin problem and is a doubt, while Trevoh Chalobah is also a fitness concern with a muscular issue.

The match takes place at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia on Wednesday, October 21, with kick-off at 5:45 pm BST.

UK viewers can watch the Champions League fixture live on TNT Sports 2 and HBO Max.

#2145. What Trey Nyoni did just before Alexander Isak goal isn’t getting enough attention

Trey Nyoni’s rise at Liverpool this season has been impressive.

The youngster’s potential was obvious to Andoni Iraola’s predecessor, Arne Slot, last term, but the club rightly decided to be careful with what was an 18-year-old footballer with limited senior experience.

It’s very much a different story in 2026/27, however, with the Basque head coach having already handed the midfielder over 600 minutes of action across the Premier League, Champions League and Carabao Cup.

If three consecutive starts for Liverpool – against Chelsea, Arsenal and Fenerbahce – doesn’t prove it, you have to think that the nature of Nyoni’s introduction against Crystal Palace more or less seals the view that Nyoni has become a key player for Iraola.

The correct decision was made ahead of time to rest the former Leicester City prodigy after a flurry of games, with our player of the season last year, Dominik Szoboszlai, restored to the starting XI.

But when it came time for the Hungarian to depart the pitch due to an injury concern, it was Trey Nyoni the manager turned to at the 69th minute mark. Yes, it’s worth pointing out that senior midfielders (other than Wataru Endo) aren’t exactly running amok on the Liverpool bench at this point in time. But this felt like a trusted substitution, a body Iraola has come to rely on, rather than one that just had to be made.

The assist credit will go to Florian Wirtz for the Alexander Isak goal close to full time, but it would be remiss of us to overlook Trey Nyoni’s contribution moments before.

The teenager can be spotted cutting out a low cross from Tyrick Mitchell in the box before picking out Alexander Isak (surrounded by three Palace players) further up the pitch. The ball plays out to Victor Munoz on the right flank, and Liverpool find a Palace defence in absolute disarray.

Isak strikes late on 💪🇸🇪 pic.twitter.com/J2fHbXlZyD

It’s not a fancy crossfield pass or Hollywood Gerrard ball, but there’s intention behind it, and it’s a positive use of the ball to help kickstart a Liverpool counter. Exactly what Andoni Iraola wants to see his midfielders doing at Liverpool on a frequent basis.

Ultimately, it’s so encouraging to see Nyoni continuing to be impactful in games even when he’s coming on, effectively, to see things out.

#2144. Rio Ferdinand disappointed Man Utd didn’t sign midfielder better than Elliot Anderson

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Rio Ferdinand has admitted he is disappointed that Manchester United failed to sign Ayyoub Bouaddi, with the highly-rated midfielder now making an impression at Manchester City.

Manchester City beat several rivals to secure Bouaddi’s signature over the summer in a deal worth £86 million. The Morocco international became the Premier League’s most expensive teenager, and Rio Ferdinand believes Manchester United missed out on a special talent.

The former United defender was particularly impressed by Bouaddi’s second-half cameo during City’s Champions League defeat to Paris Saint-Germain last week. His performance reminded Rio Ferdinand why he had been talking about the youngster throughout the previous year and during the summer transfer window.

Speaking on his YouTube channel, Ferdinand highlighted how Bouaddi was the highlight of the game against PSG and who they missed.

“He came on and showed PSG that they may have missed out on someone who was on their doorstep when he was playing in France,” Ferdinand said.

The former England international also confirmed that Manchester United had been interested in bringing the midfielder to Old Trafford, but the transfer fee was too expensive.

“Manchester United were in for him, 100%, but the fee was just too much,” he added.

Ferdinand was left frustrated by what he saw from the teenager, who turned 19 this month.

“Just that little cameo against PSG makes you go, ‘oh, I can’t believe we missed out on that. I can’t believe it.’ I’m so gutted, so gutted. He’s bad! He’s so calm under pressure.”

Rio Ferdinand suggested Bouaddi’s quality could soon make the midfield position in a packed Manchester City his regular role.

City already have Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez among their midfield options, but Ferdinand believes Bouaddi has the ability to stand out alongside them.

“They’ve got Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez in that midfield as well but this kid could be the best of the three, easily.”

#2143. Wayne Rooney drops fresh Plymouth Argyle transfer revelation

Former Plymouth Argyle head coach Wayne Rooney has dropped a fresh revelation on the transfer strategy at Home Park during his short tenure in charge of the club.

The England and Manchester United legend was relieved of his duties by the Pilgrims after a dismal run of form in the Championship during their eventual 2024/25 relegation campaign.

Argyle are currently in their second season in League One under his former Old Trafford teammate, Tom Cleverley, and after narrowly missing out on a spot in last season's play-offs, the Devon outfit are fifth in the table having won seven of their first 14 matches.

The mood at Home Park has returned to one of positivity after previous frustrations relating to on-pitch matters, whilst it has often been debated just how much longer Simon Hallet will be the man calling the shots from the boardroom.

Rooney was one of three hires made in the space of 12 months just two years ago, with his spells sandwiching those of Ian Foster and Miron Muslic.

As such, turnover was particularly rife and led to a period of significant instability, which Rooney has discussed with Ashley Young and Mark Goldbridge on the latest 'Stick to United' YouTube episode.

Plymouth have been told to demand up to £2m for Bim Pepple, who had been on the radar of Sheffield United, Bolton and Portsmouth in the summer.

Rooney's managerial career has been well-documented, particularly after underwhelming spells at Birmingham City and Plymouth, with both clubs eventually being relegated in successive seasons after parting company with the Liverpudlian.

The 41-year-old only oversaw one transfer window at Home Park, during which 11 players were recruited.

He has previously discussed the club's data-driven transfer policy during said timeframe, and did so again when analysing recent flaws at Old Trafford, which has led to sack calls emerging over former Middlesbrough boss, Michael Carrick.

Asked whether they can see similar traits between the recruitment issues during Ole Gunnar Solksjaer's time at the Red Devils and now by Young, Rooney related by saying: "I had it at Plymouth.

"It's all ran on data. You're looking at players, but then you get a couple of players in who you don't really know and haven't seen much of them.

"All of a sudden, you sign them and then you've got to try and make it work, but it's not what you identified," he added.

Argyle fans, what do you make of Wayne Rooney's comments? Comment below!

Unsurprisingly, such discourse has already alerted some on the South Coast, with one Plymouth supporter stating: Well I'm not sure about this because the start was quite good, then we had a spiral, then we had injuries and Rooney couldn't dig us out of a hole."

Another commented: That’s the Hallet way, I’m not a huge fan of it. I still believe Tom as Head Coach needs to ensure they fit in with how he wants them to play. I hope with Derek (Adams) in place this has changed."

"He sounds a bit unconvinced by his own point," another Pilgrims fan claimed.

Some were also previously unaware of such developments occurring, with another comment reading: "Didn't realise we did it purely off data that's strange."

Rooney was sacked by Plymouth after winning just five of his 20 matches in charge, but almost two years later, even though the club is in a lower division, such times are firmly behind its supporters.

Instead, they have certainly been encouraged by the form showcased by Cleverley's side in recent weeks and months, particularly after the Argyle bosses' response to last season's alarming start.
