#2142. Michael Carrick handed lifeline ahead of Chelsea trip

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick has come under immense pressure as a disjointed Manchester United sit just two places above the relegation places.

Despite the scrutiny and the news of a possible sacking, Michael Carrick has been given another chance at Manchester United.

Michael Carrick will remain in charge of Manchester United for their Premier League trip to Chelsea on Saturday after the club decided to give their under-pressure head coach another opportunity to turn things around.

United have gone eight games without a win in all competitions, with Sunday’s humiliating 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth leaving them 16th in the Premier League table, just one point above the relegation zone.

Carrick’s position looked increasingly uncertain after the Bournemouth loss. As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were at Carrington on Monday to discuss the situation.

It has been reported by Sky Sports that United have considered other options internally as well to replace Micahel Carrick but for the weekend, they are sticking with Michael Carrick.

Carrick took training on Tuesday and the decision means he has at least one more match to convince the club’s hierarchy that he can get United back on track.

Gary Neville admitted he was surprised that Carrick had survived the immediate threat of losing his job, although he welcomed the decision.

“I am surprised, but I am happy because I was absolutely certain that what I witnessed on Sunday would be the end of Michael,” Neville said in Sky Sports.

The former United captain explained that Carrick’s actions after the Bournemouth defeat had made him think the manager was preparing to say goodbye to the supporters.

“It looked like he was walking round at the end on his own with the players behind him, like it was almost a goodbye lap,” Neville added.

Fans continued singing the manager’s name after the final whistle, while the rest of the team drew boos.

Despite his surprise, Gary Neville hopes Micahel Carrick can use the extra time to get a response from his squad.

“I hope he can somehow get those players to start playing for him again because they haven’t played for him in the last few weeks,” he said.

The trip to Chelsea now becomes a crucial test for Micahel Carrick but United need a result and a far better performance to ease the pressure at Old Trafford.

#2141. Two days or two weeks – Michael Carrick future decision rolls into the international break

Manchester United will use the November international break to decide whether Michael Carrick stays on as head coach, after a three-game reprieve that delivered only one win.

According to The Guardian, Carrick came close to being sacked after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October.

Director of football Jason Wilcox and chief executive Omar Berrada discussed his position at the training ground the following day and agreed to give him three matches to turn things around before the break.

Those games brought a 2-0 defeat at Chelsea, a 3-2 win over Roma and a chaotic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa at Old Trafford on Sunday. United conceded eight goals across the three fixtures, and defensive frailty remains the biggest worry.

Wilcox is now leading a review of Carrick’s position. He will consult Berrada before making a recommendation to Sir Jim Ratcliffe, who oversees the club’s sporting operations. Talks are expected to begin on Monday and continue through the break.

The league table makes grim reading. United sit in the Premier League relegation zone with just one league win in their last 10 matches.

If he does win a stay of execution, results will need to follow. Manchester United cannot be languishing in 17th place.

Carrick remains defiant. Speaking to Sky Sports News after the Villa draw, he admitted his side “haven’t won enough games” but insisted he expects to move forward.

“I’m quite clear with what direction the club needs to go in, and it’s got to win games, said Carrick after the late Villa draw.

“I think there’s more than enough [to turn things around].”

If United do make a change, former Newcastle manager Eddie Howe is reported to be Wilcox’s preferred candidate. Howe has been out of work since leaving St James’ Park during pre-season, after four and a half years in charge.

In the meantime, there are no plans to strengthen Carrick’s coaching staff.

For now, Carrick is expected to be in charge for United’s next match, against Liverpool. Whether three games were enough to convince the hierarchy should become clear before then.

The trip to Anfield could be do or die for the decorated former United midfielder.

He’s not likely to survive a heavy defeat at the home of the club’s fierce rivals, but a galvanising win would surely buy him time and goodwill.

That’s if he makes it through the next fortnight before then. A decision or indication could be two days or two weeks away.

The only certainty at Old Trafford is uncertainty.

#2140. Nottingham Forest told to 'cough up' minimum '£15m' for Birmingham City transfer

Birmingham City have been urged to demand a strong fee after transfer speculation involving German centre-back, Phil Neumann has come to light once again during the November international break.

The 29-year-old centre-back has been a key reason as to why Chris Davies' side are topping the Championship table after 15 games played.

Blues' record of just 14 concessions during said timeframe is the best in the division as they sit ahead of Middlesbrough and Stoke City on goal difference.

However, transfer speculation at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park has never been too far away, such is the ambition showcased in this part of the world.

That said, it would be a huge blow for the club if they were to lose the imperious defender's services, and exactly why Football League World's Birmingham City fan pundit, Jason Moore, believes at least £15m should be demanded as a result.

Neumann is now in the thick of his second Championship campaign, having officially signed for Blues last July after striking a pre-contract agreement during his final months with Hannover 96.

Despite an indifferent first season in English football, the 6"3 figure was the subject of reported interest from Serie A side, Sassuolo, in January, although a swift exit would not materialise.

A clear trend has developed involving Tom Wagner and Jay Stansfield at Birmingham City.

Neumann then reaffirmed in August that his main aim was to help Birmingham's promotion prospects after further links with Hull City and Borussia Mönchengladbach, whilst Sky Deutschland now claim that Nottingham Forest and Fiorentina are admirers of the former youth international.

It has also been suggested that a move could happen in January, with the defender's valuation being between €5m and €10m.

Neumann has been an ever-present for Birmingham this season, with his 15 appearances in the league helping him surpass 50 in total for the club.

As well as aiding Davies' side's stellar defensive record, the defender's brace at Wrexham in August was critical in a timely success following a 6-1 thrashing in the EFL Cup against Brentford three days prior, which increased the heat on the Blues boss at the time.

Such is his importance at the spine of Blues' side, the aforementioned Moore made his feelings clear over this transfer saga when asked by FLW.

"It's got to be £15m at the bare minimum," he said.

"The guy is an absolute form of his life, and there's an argument there that he's been the best centre-half in the division. We've probably got the best centre-half pairing in the entire division as well.

"£15m, and I'm only saying £15m because of his age, so cough it up. But, for me, he doesn't go anywhere," Moore claimed.

Blues fans, what is Phil Neumann's current valuation in your view? Comment below!

"He's too vital to what we're doing, and currently we look like we're in a good position to potentially try and go up. I just don't see a world where we'd even entertain it unless someone's going to cough up some serious money for him.

"For me, it has to be a minimum of £15m. If someone's not going to cough that up, then no."

Neumann is under contract until June 2028, meaning Birmingham will hold the majority of the cards when it comes to deciding the defender's short-term future.

Having never played in any top-flight thus far, it would be no surprise if such a move perked the defender's interest, but there is certainly a chance that such feats could transpire in B9, where he is likely to continue receiving more gametime.

#2139. Monaco summit adds unnecessary drama to Michael Carrick situation

Michael Carrick arrived at Carrington for Manchester United training as normal on Tuesday morning, while his bosses were preparing to decide his future in talks on the Mediterranean.

Chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox are flying to Monaco to meet Sir Jim Ratcliffe, with the BBC reporting that the pair will recommend Carrick is sacked.The Guardian has indicated representatives of the Glazer family will also be involved.

The club insists the Monaco summit was pre-planned and is not solely about the head coach and while that may be true, it hardly matters.By allowing the meeting to become public knowledge, United have turned a straightforward football decision into unnecessary drama, compounding an already difficult situation.

What does is say about Man United in 2026 that a manager is given three games to secure his job, then left to train the squad during an international break while the executives are flying off to rule on his future? It’s farcical.

The latest chapter is another muddled process.Wilcox and Berrada met at Carrington the day after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October and agreed to give Carrick three more matches.A defeat at Chelsea, a win over Roma and a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa later, they appear to have reached the conclusion many expected a fortnight ago.

If the timeline holds, the decision will be made at Wednesday’s meeting, with an official announcement expected later in the week.United reportedly want to tell Carrick face to face, which means he could spend another day or two working under a cloud. In reality, it could be two days or two weeks.

The stakes could hardly be higher. United have won once in 10 league games, their worst-ever start to a Premier League season, and sit one point off the bottom in 18th place.

Should Carrick go, attention will turn quickly to his successor.ESPN reports that United States head coach Mauricio Pochettino is the favoured candidate, while Eddie Howe has been named as Wilcox’s preferred choice. Antonio Conte has also been mentioned.

Carrick deserves better than this.Whatever the outcome, a club that wants to be taken seriously should not be settling its manager’s future in the full glare of the media while he is still out on the training pitch.

#2138. “I’m really upset” – One thing hugely annoyed Andoni Iraola despite Liverpool win on Sunday

Andoni Iraola was left ‘really upset’ after Liverpool’s win over Crystal Palace on Sunday despite the positive, morale-boosting result for his team.

Having begun November with defeats to Arsenal and Fenerbahce, the Reds went to Selhurst Park in search of a pick-me-up in their final game prior to the international window, and they duly got in the form of a 3-1 victory.

Goals from Alexis Mac Allister, Florian Wirtz and Alexander Isak had the points secured well before a stoppage-time penalty when, in a clash of heads between Milos Kerkez and Dario Osorio, the Hungarian was deemed to be the perpetrator and referee Tony Harrington pointed to the spot.

Eddie Nketiah converted from 12 yards to deny Liverpool a fifth Premier League clean sheet of the campaign, and speaking to Sky Sports shortly after the final whistle, Iraola’s frustration over the awarding of the penalty was clear.

The Basque said: “I’m very upset [with] what they are calling in our box penalties, and the ones they’re not calling in the other box. I know that we are 3-0, it’s the 95th minute, but I’m very upset.

“It’s a head-to-head but even Osorio’s head, after touching the ball, is the one going to Milos. It’s not Milos to Osorio. A clash of heads.

“OK, you can say 3-0, 95 minutes, nothing happens, but for me there is more than this. There is more because it’s not the first time this season and you compare and I’m really upset, yes.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

Thankfully the decision came at a point in the match where it had no bearing on the overall result, although we can still see why it put a dampener on the victory for Iraola.

To us, it seemed like a 50-50 contest between Kerkez and Osorio, with both players making a genuine attempt to win the ball and unfortunate to have clashed heads – most importantly, both were fine after the collision.

Harrington’s call didn’t have any major impact in the overall context of the day, but we take the Liverpool head coach’s point about the apparent inconsistency in terms of what referees deem to be a penalty and what they don’t.

There was certainly a far stronger case for a spot kick in our defeat to Arsenal last weekend when Jurrien Timber blatantly tugged Isak’s shirt inside the box, but somehow none of the officials on duty thought it was a foul.

It didn’t hurt the Reds yesterday, but hopefully on another occasion where there’s a stoppage-time penalty given in a game which is in the balance, we won’t end up having to talk about Iraola’s side being wronged by the referee and VAR.

#2136. Moises Caicedo And Kendry Paez Give Chelsea World Cup Signal

Ecuador’s late World Cup surge has given Chelsea a useful development signal through Moises Caicedo and Kendry Paez before Xabi Alonso’s first pre-season friendly on 28 July.

Chelsea confirmed that Caicedo and Paez reached the Round of 32 after Ecuador’s dramatic 2-1 win over Germany.

For Alonso, the contrast between the two Chelsea midfielders matters as much as the result.

Caicedo captained Ecuador through the pressure game, with We Ain’t Got No History highlighting the Chelsea midfielder’s leadership role in a win that rescued their tournament.

Paez, meanwhile, sits in a different phase of his rise.

That should not worry Chelsea. FIFA identified Paez as one of the tournament’s wonderkids, but Chelsea’s bigger win is that he can absorb elite-tournament preparation without carrying a defining role too soon.

Read Chelsea has already looked at how Alonso must handle Paez’s development curve, and this knockout place sharpens that argument.

Alonso inherits a midfield loaded with senior names, transfer noise and immediate pressure.

Caicedo gives Chelsea the proven pillar. Paez gives them the controlled upside.

Ecuador’s run now gives Chelsea both at once.

For Chelsea, that is the ideal succession picture: leadership today, patience for tomorrow.

#2135. Arsenal news: Arteta’s side secure landmark victory at Anfield

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

In Arsenal news, Arsenal have finally found a way to win at Anfield again. Their latest victory owed as much to defensive discipline as Kai Havertz’s decisive goal.

The Gunners claimed a 1-0 Premier League victory against Liverpool, ending their 14-year wait for victory at the iconic stadium. Declan Rice set up their opener as Havertz put the visitors ahead on the 16th minute.

It was a tough afternoon for Mikel Arteta’s side as they were put under sustained pressure by their opponents. The Reds also created a number of dangerous chances throughout the match, especially in the first half.

Arsenal held their composure, and the backline was calm throughout. David Raya made the crucial interventions to keep the lead safe.

They are now three points ahead at the top of the Premier League after Manchester City lost to Brighton.

While the attack has been a key aspect of Arsenal’s title charge, they were put under pressure defensively at Anfield.

Liverpool came out aggressively after going behind. Virgil van Dijk caused problems from set pieces, and Florian Wirtz hit the crossbar from a free kick.

Raya was quick to block Van Dijk’s dangerous flick on and prevent Alexander Isak from capitalising. However, Alexis Mac Allister did head over the bar as Liverpool looked to level the game.

Arsenal were forced into another stretch as Jurrien Timber blocked well. William Saliba and Gabriel helped to limit Liverpool’s chances.

The stats tell the story of how well the visitors defended. Liverpool had 17 shots but couldn’t get one on target.

Liverpool were unable to create a shot on target for the first time in recorded history (since 2003-04) in a Premier League game at Anfield.

Meanwhile, it was the first time that Arsenal had kept a clean sheet in the Premier League at the ground since September 2012. The result was especially important following their Carabao Cup heartbreak against Fleetwood Town earlier in the week.

Whilst they have made a great defensive stand, Arsenal’s two most influential players in the final third made the difference.

Rice won the turnover, drove down the right and played a low cross into the six-yard box. Havertz made his run to the back post and finished to give Arsenal the early lead.

The goal also takes Havertz to 46 Premier League goals, making him the most prolific German in the competition and overtaking former Manchester City midfielder Ilkay Gündogan.

Rice had a huge influence on the game. His performance in the middle of the park helped Arsenal deal with the intensity of Liverpool, and he was named man of the match.

Christos Tzolis provided an important attacking outlet. His pace created chances to ease pressure on Arsenal. He forced an incredible save from Alisson with a late first-time effort.

The win was Arsenal’s first at Anfield in any competition since September 2012 and their 300th away win in the Premier League.

Arteta’s team have had a stellar start to the season, taking 24 points from nine games. They have also proved they are capable of getting results in tough circumstances.

They will now look ahead to their Champions League game on Wednesday against Slavia Prague and then back to domestic action against Hull City.

#2134. Chelsea World Cup Knockout Load Gives Xabi Alonso Pre-Season Problem

Chelsea’s World Cup contingent has moved from a group-stage watchlist into a genuine Xabi Alonso planning problem.

Chelsea’s own tournament update confirms that Enzo Fernandez, Mike Penders, Moises Caicedo, Kendry Paez, Malo Gusto and Jorrel Hato have reached the Round of 32.

England have also progressed after their final Group L win over Panama, which keeps Reece James and Trevoh Chalobah inside the wider knockout picture.

For supporters, that is a status marker. For Alonso, it is where summer pride starts to become pre-season compression.

The concern is not simply volume. It is where the minutes are landing.

Enzo has not missed a minute for Argentina, while Caicedo has remained central for Ecuador and captained them in the 2-1 win over Germany.

Those are not fringe workloads. They are the two midfield profiles Chelsea have built large parts of their control game around.

Alonso’s first Chelsea pre-season was always going to demand clarity through the middle of the pitch.

Instead, the players most central to that reset are still operating in knockout football.

Caicedo’s Ecuador role is the cleanest example. Read Chelsea has already assessed how his captaincy display against Germany strengthened his leadership case, but the next layer is more complicated.

Every additional knockout tie gives him more tournament rhythm, more authority and more fatigue.

Argentina’s route against Cape Verde keeps him on a glamorous stage at exactly the moment Chelsea need to settle his role, mood and tactical responsibilities under Alonso.

If Madrid-related noise continues around him, the club cannot afford a sluggish July handover.

Reece James started England’s first two matches before a hamstring issue ruled him out of the Panama game, according to the Guardian.

England still topped the group and moved into a last-32 tie with DR Congo, but James’ situation is exactly the kind of detail Chelsea’s medical team will monitor closely.

Chelsea have already had to plan around a large World Cup group.

The broader issue was clear in Read Chelsea’s earlier pre-season workload piece, but the knockout stage sharpens it because the returns will now be staggered.

Enzo brings a maximum group-stage load. Caicedo brings captaincy responsibility and heavy Ecuador minutes.

James brings knockout qualification mixed with another hamstring-management question.

Gusto and Hato remain tied to France and the Netherlands, while Penders and Paez are still absorbing tournament environments before club reintegration.

That is a spine issue, not a fringe issue.

Alonso needs automatisms in possession, spacing around the double pivot, clearer full-back behaviour and pressing triggers from midfield.

Those details do not bed in properly when key players arrive in drips after a major summer tournament.

Players returning from knockout football can bring competitive sharpness that closed-door friendlies cannot replicate.

Caicedo captaining Ecuador through pressure games matters. Enzo playing high-leverage minutes for Argentina matters.

Gusto and Hato absorbing tournament detail also helps Chelsea’s defensive reset.

But Chelsea cannot treat every tournament minute as clean preparation.

Alonso must decide who returns straight into tactical work, who needs a genuine pause and who can handle August without creating an avoidable injury bill by September.

That is where the James situation feels central.

If Chelsea’s captain carries a World Cup hamstring warning, the club’s right-sided structure becomes more than a selection debate.

It becomes an availability question that touches Gusto, the wider defensive rebuild and the need for control from midfield.

Read Chelsea has already looked at James’ captaincy test under Alonso, and the injury layer now makes that test sharper.

This is the hidden cost of a strong international footprint.

Chelsea want players good enough to survive deep into tournaments.

Alonso now has to make sure that status does not leave his first league weeks short of the very control those players are meant to provide.

#2133. Arsenal injury news: Eberechi Eze concern emerges as Bukayo Saka awaits scan

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal came out on top in their match against Liverpool. Mikel Arteta admitted they have a new injury concern to deal with after Eberechi Eze was hurt. Bukayo Saka is also still out for the Gunners as they face two more games before the break.

The 1-0 victory over Liverpool at Anfield means they are three points clear at the top of the Premier League. Arteta will need to be careful with his squad ahead of their Champions League clash against Slavia Prague on Wednesday and home game against Hull City.

Eze was a surprise omission from the starting line-up, despite featuring well in recent games.

Arteta later revealed that the England international was suffering from a problem going into the game. He also praised the club’s medical and performance team for getting him fit enough to be included in the travelling party.

There is some light at the end of the tunnel, with Eze being named on the bench. There is uncertainty over whether he will be available for the trip to Prague.

Arsenal will look to train him to see if he can play against Slavia. However, Arteta’s comments suggest that the issue is significant enough that he couldn’t start against Anfield despite his recent form.

There is positive news on Declan Rice, Noni Madueke and Riccardo Calafiori. All three were able to overcome their fitness concerns to start the game against Liverpool and play the full 90 minutes.

The availability of these players provides Arteta with more options as he heads into a busy week. However, he will be keen to avoid any unnecessary risk to those that have only just returned to full fitness.

Saka is still the biggest injury concern for Arsenal as he has failed to play for the last three games after suffering a quad injury in the clash against Bayern Munich.

He was forced off after just 10 minutes of the Champions League draw. He will not be available for the fixtures against Slavia Prague and Hull.

There were concerns that Saka may miss up to eight weeks of action. Arteta hasn’t confirmed when he will be back on the pitch.

The Arsenal manager explained earlier that there was a need to scan Saka again to see how the fluid around the injury was behaving. The medical staff will also want to know if there has been any damage to the tendon.

This will give clearer information on when he will be able to return to play. Until then Arsenal will have to plan without one of their most important attacking players.

Another player looking to make his way back to fitness is William Saliba. The defender has not played this season after aggravating a back issue during his time playing for France at the World Cup.

However, he has avoided surgery and has been back in individual outdoor training at the club’s training ground recently. Arteta says there are positive signs that his recovery is going well.

The club plan for an MRI scan to see how he is recovering. They are hoping to get him back in December, but it has not been confirmed by Arsenal.

It would give Arteta a huge boost to his defensive options. For now he will have to keep monitoring his recovery.

They play Slavia Prague on Wednesday and then play Hull at home on Saturday. This gives them two more chances to build up their position ahead of the international break; however, Arteta will have to consider the latest medical reports.

#2132. Malo Gusto’s Kante Lesson Gives Chelsea A £75m Xabi Alonso Decision

Malo Gusto’s World Cup has suddenly become more than a France subplot for Chelsea. It is now a live assessment of whether Xabi Alonso inherits a right-back worth protecting, reshaping or trading at the peak of his market.

Chelsea’s own interview with Gusto this week underlined the human part of that equation. The 23-year-old spoke about learning from N’Golo Kante, calling the former Blue one of the best midfielders France have produced and stressing the value of his example on and off the pitch.

That matters because Kante is not just a nostalgic reference point. He remains the standard Chelsea supporters still use when judging humility, intensity and reliability in a high-pressure dressing room.

For Gusto, those qualities arrive at a decisive moment. Reports around the player’s future claim Chelsea value him at around £75million, with the imminent Marco Palestra deal creating a more crowded full-back picture.

That number looks aggressive, but the logic is clear. Gusto is young, capped by France, under contract, Premier League-tested and capable of playing in several defensive structures.

🚨🚨| NEW: Chelsea are open to selling Malo Gusto this summer, with his agent already in 𝐓𝐀𝐋𝐊𝐒 with Manchester City and Inter. Chelsea will only sell if their valuation is met.{@siphillipssport} pic.twitter.com/vNR2bWud9F

The easiest reading of Gusto’s Kante comments is sentimental. It should not be.

For Alonso, who officially begins work on July 1, the important detail is what those comments say about the player Chelsea have been developing.

Gusto has not always looked completely settled at Stamford Bridge. His best games have carried thrust, speed and recovery power, while his less convincing spells have raised questions about final-ball quality and defensive concentration.

That uncertainty is exactly why the next coach matters.

Alonso’s previous teams have leaned heavily on wide players who can receive under pressure, attack space early and still protect the back line when possession breaks down. Gusto has the athletic base for that job.

The test is whether his decision-making can be refined enough to make him a structural piece rather than a rotation asset.

That is where the Kante reference lands with force. Chelsea do not need Gusto to imitate Kante’s position. They need him to absorb the same professional standard: repeat actions, clean defensive choices, no wasted emotion and total commitment to the collective rhythm.

The Marco Palestra deal changes the right side of Chelsea’s squad immediately. Read Chelsea has already examined how Palestra’s arrival offers a tactical clue over Alonso’s defensive plans, and that pressure is now sharpened by market noise around Gusto.

Chelsea can frame the situation in two ways.

One is profit-led. If a club comes close to the reported £75million level, selling a player signed from Lyon for a much lower fee becomes a powerful PSR lever.

The other is football-led. If Alonso sees Gusto as a high-upside wing-back who fits his model, selling him just as the coaching environment becomes more suitable would carry long-term risk.

The complication is Reece James. The captain’s fitness issues, including his current England hamstring concern, mean Chelsea still need genuine right-sided depth.

Palestra may be part of the future, but Gusto is already part of the present. Chelsea have too often needed emergency solutions in that area to treat proven depth lightly.

That is why Read Chelsea’s recent look at James awaiting hamstring scan results matters in this debate. Right-back remains one of the most talented areas of the squad, but also one of the least straightforward.

This is why Gusto’s France rhythm is more valuable than a simple tournament diary entry. Chelsea are watching a player operate inside an elite international camp, learn from a club legend and handle a public transfer valuation that can change his status overnight.

Alonso’s first decision does not have to be final, but it does have to be clear.

If Gusto stays, he needs a defined role early in pre-season and a pathway that makes sense alongside Palestra and James. If Chelsea sell, they need to do it at a number that justifies weakening one of the few squad areas where youth, speed and top-level experience already overlap.

Gusto’s Kante lesson, then, is not just a feel-good Chelsea connection. It is a reminder of the standard Alonso must demand before deciding whether £75million is a price to accept or a warning sign that Chelsea should hold firm.

#2131. Exclusive: Insider’s Verdict On ‘Brighton Star To West Ham’ Transfer Claim

One player who was reportedly linked with a move to West Ham United during the summer transfer window was Brighton & Hove Albion winger Amario Cozier-Duberry.

The 21-year-old joined Middlesbrough on a season-long loan after signing a new three-year contract with the Seagulls earlier this summer.

Cozier-Duberry has made an impressive start to life at the Riverside Stadium, registering three goals and five assists in just ten league appearances.

West Ham strengthened their wide options following the departure of Crysencio Summerville, bringing in Edvin Austbø and Manor Solomon. However, as Claret & Hugh can exclusively reveal, reports suggesting the Hammers submitted either a loan or permanent offer for Cozier-Duberry were inaccurate.

When approached for clarification, a trusted transfer source provided a brief but unequivocal response: “Not true.”

West Ham settled on Austbø – and appear to have done pretty well by signing the up and coming young winger

The winger attracted interest from several clubs during the summer, including Rangers, as uncertainty surrounded his future at Brighton following the club’s reported £21 million investment in Zadock Yohanna.

Before joining Middlesbrough, Cozier-Duberry enjoyed an outstanding loan spell with Bolton Wanderers in League One last season. He scored 11 goals and contributed 13 assists, underlining his reputation as one of the country’s most promising young attacking talents.

At West Ham, Manor Solomon has experienced a mixed start to his spell in east London, while Austbø has already chipped in with two goals and two assists despite largely being used as a substitute.

It will be fascinating to see how Cozier-Duberry’s loan campaign at Middlesbrough develops over the course of the season. However, any suggestion that he was close to wearing a claret-and-blue shirt appears to be wide of the mark.

#2130. Ronald Araujo agrees with Liverpool’s position on his £47m buy option

Liverpool would prefer to wait until the end of the season before deciding whether or not to activate Ronald Araujo’s £47m buy option.

That’s the claim coming out of SPORT, with the defender himself also understood to prefer delaying a decision until the summer.

According to the outlet in question, the Reds are thought to be “very pleased” with the Uruguayan international. However, the club is very aware of the fact that it will take a somewhat significant figure to bring the centre-back to Anfield on a permanent basis.

But barring a sudden, unexpected collapse of truly cosmic proportions, it’s difficult to see how Liverpool could manage to justify not activating the buy option come the summer.

Even if the club’s long-term plan is to use a less conservative option at right-back, the fact of the matter is they’re going to be quite short on centre-back options come the end of the season.

Club skipper Virgil van Dijk (contracted until 2027) may yet sign an extension, but we can’t see Joe Gomez staying put beyond the summer window. Either way, and even assuming that Giovanni Leoni returns from his ACL injury in excellent shape and has a significant second half of the season at L4, the Reds will need to bolster the department to the tune of potentially two bodies.

Activating Araujo’s buy option, then, feels like a necessity rather than a luxury, especially given the experience that could walk out of the door at the end of the campaign.

SPORT go on to claim that Araujo “is aware that Barcelona is keeping a close eye on him”, which doesn’t really communicate a great deal as far as intention is concerned.

Prior reporting has indicated that Barca boss Hansi Flick will hold some sway in the player’s next move this summer, but ultimately, it should be emphasised that Liverpool Football Club handed the footballer a new lease of life. Not Barcelona. And we’d like to think that’ll count for something in the long run.

Traditionalists will likely be aghast at the thought of fans undermining their own team in this manner, whilst those who participated no doubt feel it was the only way to show their contempt for the perceived lack of commitment and effort which those eleven representatives  of the Claret and Blue heritage displayed.

Not to mention the hundreds of pounds it costs each supporter, every game, merely to arrive at the turnstiles in support of our club. But is it the ‘right’ thing to do?

And I’d not even heard ‘you’re not fit to wear the shirt‘ sung by West Ham fans in my couple of decades’ worth of attending games.

#2128. Arsenal transfer news: Arteta considers January moves for two highly rated attackers

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal could have two ambitious attacking signings in mind for January. Eli Junior Kroupi and Kenan Yildiz are reportedly attracting Mikel Arteta’s attention.

Both players have emerged as potential targets as the Gunners consider further changes to their forward line. However, Arsenal may face a battle to prise either youngster away from their current club.

Kroupi is already familiar with the Premier League after making an impressive start to life at Bournemouth. The forward arrived from Lorient and scored 13 goals in 35 appearances across all competitions during his first season in England.

His development was halted by a broken foot in pre-season which required an operation to fix and kept him out of action for around three months.

The 20-year-old has now returned to the Bournemouth squad and has played as a substitute in their last three games. He will be hoping to get more game time as he gets back into full fitness.

Arsenal were linked with a move for Kroupi in the summer. His return to action has reignited interest in the player. A number of other European clubs also understood to be watching his situation.

Kroupi has attracted interest from a variety of top clubs including Manchester United, Tottenham, Bayern Munich and Paris Saint-Germain.

The French Under-21 international has impressed with his goal scoring in his first campaign in England. At only 20 years old, he has plenty of development to do under an experienced manager like Arteta.

However, Bournemouth are said to be reluctant to let Kroupi leave in January. Selling a bright young forward would leave Marco Rose with a tough decision to make.

It could get even more complicated if Alex Scott is also being seriously pursued. Chelsea and Liverpool are both linked with the midfielder.

Arsenal may therefore struggle to convince the Cherries to part with Kroupi during the winter window.

Another exciting option for Arsenal comes in the form of Juventus winger Yildiz. The winger has been linked with a move to North London for a number of transfer windows. He has once again been in the news recently at the end of the summer.

However, the Turkish international has struggled to get going on this campaign. He has only featured once in Serie A and has failed to feature since he suffered an injury.

Juventus have previously shown little interest in letting him leave. Head coach Luciano Spalletti firmly dismissed the prospect during the summer. Indicating that even an offer worth €100 million would not change the club’s position.

However, things could change next season. There are reports from Italy that Juventus may have to sell their star player if they fail to make the Champions League.

The Turin-based outfit are currently sitting in seventh spot in Serie A after just 10 games and are six points away from the qualifying spots.

This means that if they don’t make it into Europe, then it could put more pressure on Arsenal’s pursuit of Yildiz. It could create more pressure on them to raise funds.

Arsenal had a very busy summer with the addition of Bruno Guimaraes, Christos Tzolis, Ezri Konsa, Piero Hincapie and Illan Meslier.

But they also missed out on a few big names, such as Vinicius Jr and Julian Alvarez.

Despite this, there is still interest in Kroupi and Yildiz, suggesting that Arteta is still looking to strengthen his attacking options. Both players would bring quality and potential to the squad; however, there are some issues with both.

There are many unknowns surrounding this situation. Bournemouth aren’t keen to sell Kroupi and Juventus aren’t in a great financial position.

This doesn’t mean that either player will definitely be moving to the Emirates but much will depend on what happens at their clubs in the coming months.

#2127. Senegal Knockout Tie Gives Chelsea A Double Xabi Alonso Audit

Chelsea’s World Cup watch has moved beyond simple minutes management.

Senegal’s Round-of-32 meeting with Belgium now gives Xabi Alonso a live read on two very different development tracks.

Nicolas Jackson’s attacking authority is the immediate question. Mamadou Sarr’s defensive readiness is the longer-term one.

Chelsea confirmed that Senegal kept their knockout hopes alive with a 5-0 win over Iraq, with Jackson featuring from the bench.

Senegal have since moved into the knockouts, where they face Belgium in Seattle on Wednesday 1 July.

Jackson and Sarr travel into that game with contrasting club questions attached. Both still carry clear Chelsea relevance.

Sarr has not been the loudest Chelsea story of the tournament.

That may actually make this test more useful.

The 20-year-old is still moving through the awkward stage between prospect, squad option and saleable BlueCo asset.

Knockout football against Belgium can sharpen that picture quickly.

The timing is awkward in the best possible way for Chelsea. Alonso steps into a squad with expensive centre-backs, academy-trained options and a board still comfortable using the market aggressively.

A disciplined Sarr performance would not end that search. It would give the recruitment team a stronger internal benchmark before committing another major fee.

Chelsea need defenders who can hold a high line, cover wide channels and still pass through pressure. Belgium should ask those questions more directly than most group-stage opponents.

The context matters. Chelsea’s recall note recorded that Sarr returned from Strasbourg after 18 appearances across Ligue 1 and the Conference League.

The club also noted that he had already earned his first Senegal cap.

That is a different profile from an academy player being carried by reputation alone.

There is a statistical base behind the interest too. When Chelsea completed the deal, beIN SPORTS noted that Sarr made 27 Ligue 1 appearances in his first Strasbourg season.

He helped them keep 10 clean sheets. He also produced a team-high 20 blocks and made 25 interceptions.

Those numbers explain why this Belgium game is not just a sentimental World Cup subplot.

Alonso’s Chelsea defence is already crowded with Levi Colwill, Wesley Fofana, Trevoh Chalobah and external targets still orbiting the plan.

ReadChelsea has already looked at why Colwill’s recovery story gives Alonso a defensive test. Sarr’s value is different.

He is still being evaluated as a physical, long-range defensive project.

Jackson’s role changes the editorial weight of the tie.

Chelsea’s striker picture has been messy for long enough. Any high-pressure international performance now carries wider meaning.

ReadChelsea has already framed the forward’s tournament as a live Senegal striker audit for Chelsea.

Belgium should stress both Chelsea questions at once.

Jackson has to offer penalty-box threat and transition security. Sarr, if trusted, has to defend space against a technically cleaner opponent.

He also has to show whether his recovery pace and frame translate under tournament pressure.

Sarr’s own mentality profile is worth noting. In a Chelsea World Cup Q&A, he named Lionel Messi, Sadio Mane and Paul Pogba as his tournament heroes.

His answer on Pogba focused on leadership and group mentality.

That lands neatly with the exact question Chelsea now need answered. Is there enough presence to go with the physical tools?

This is why Belgium feels like a pathway check rather than a routine World Cup watch item.

Chelsea do not need Sarr to solve the defence in July. They need clearer evidence on whether he belongs in Alonso’s first pre-season group.

He could need another loan. He could also become part of the club’s centre-back market arithmetic.

For Jackson, the question is more immediate. For Sarr, it is more structural.

Together, Senegal’s knockout tie gives Chelsea a rare clean look at two players on opposite ends of the same squad-building argument.

Who can help now, and who is worth protecting for later?

#2126. Arsenal U19s face tough Slavia Prague test in UEFA Youth League

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal’s under-19s face a significant challenge when they travel to Czechia to take on Slavia Prague in the UEFA Youth League on Wednesday.

The young Gunners have picked up four points from their first three games and will be hoping to build on that in the league phase. Two wins would definitely help their chances of getting into the knockout stages.

They kicked off their campaign with a win against Napoli and put on a great show in a 3-3 draw against Bayern Munich last month.

Those results have left them 18th in the standings, 16 places below their next opponents. With the top 22 teams progressing to the knockout stages, another positive result could prove important for their qualification hopes.

However, Slavia Prague has made a much stronger start and will provide a difficult examination for David Horseman’s side.

Slavia have won all three of their first games in the UEFA Youth League and are sitting in second place. Just behind Real Madrid on goal difference.

They began well with a 2-1 win against French side Lens. They then went on to beat Sabah 3-0 and then put in another strong display against Fenerbahce.

Despite falling behind in Prague, the players fought back to win the game 3-1. The three wins means Slavia are already past the number of points they scored last season in the league stage.

They also advanced to the knockout stages last time, finishing 20th before they faced Benfica.

Mubarak Suleiman put the team ahead twice, but they were pegged back by two goals in the last 30 minutes as their European run came to an end.

This season they have looked like a much stronger team. With a perfect record, they will be one of the hardest teams that Arsenal will face.

The meeting on Wednesday provides an opportunity for Arsenal to get revenge for their heavy defeat in Prague last season.

The two sides met during the 2025/26 league campaign, with Slavia winning 5-1 at home.

Arsenal were three goals down at the break after an own goal plus goals from Bartosz Szywala and Jakub Kolisek.

O’Neill pulled one back after the break, but the home side had the final word as Suleiman and Matej Kotrba scored in stoppage time.

This loss shows how tough it will be for Horseman’s side. Slavia have already proven their attacking prowess against Arsenal and are playing even better in this campaign.

The young Gunners will have to play much more disciplined if they are to leave Prague with three points.

Arsenal fans can watch the UEFA Youth League clash on Wednesday.

It will be streamed via the Arsenal app and Arsenal.com, with the match taking place at the Sports Centre Radotin.

Updates will also be posted here and on the club’s Academy X page.

A full match report, highlights and post-match interviews with Horseman and one of the young players will be published on Arsenal’s official channels.

With them still in contention to qualify for the knockout stages, Arsenal will be looking to get off to a good start. Slavia’s great early start means they will have to play some of their best football of the tournament.

#2125. “He’s Got The Yips” Mads Hermansen’s West Ham Nightmare Goes Beyond Bad Form

There is a phenomenon in sport known as the yips. It’s a condition which seems to affect previously capable sportsmen, psychologically blocking them from performing even the simplest of tasks. In darts, it can become difficult to release the dart from the hand, while in golf it can make putting almost impossible.

In essence, you’re frozen, seemingly unable to perform a task which should have become second nature. And it was during Blackburn Rovers’ second goal against West Ham yesterday, when I watched Mads Hermansen fail to dive, that I found myself wondering whether this goalkeeper has developed the footballing equivalent of the yips.

It wasn’t just that goal, of course. The first was equally concerning, when Ezra Mayers was beaten down the flank and the ball was crossed into the penalty area. The situation required some kind of intervention from Hermansen, but there was none. No punch, no parry, no save. Just indecision and a complete lack of intervention.

Unfortunately, this apparent hesitation has permeated Hermansen’s game recently and it’s hard not to conclude that he’s got the yips. There have been occasions when he has looked like little more than a bystander as goals have flown past him, sometimes appearing rooted to the spot or simply falling over rather than making a convincing attempt to save the ball.

The statistics are equally worrying. Based on the match figures we’ve examined, Hermansen has saved just 20% of the shots on target he has faced over West Ham’s last three Championship matches, conceding eight goals from ten attempts on target.

That’s an extraordinary return for a goalkeeper at any level, let alone one playing for a club with West Ham’s promotion ambitions. And while defenders must accept their share of the responsibility, the goalkeeper ultimately has to provide some resistance when opponents get a shot away.

The warning signs were already there before Cardiff, and his latest performance has only increased my concerns. I don’t know whether Hermansen is suffering from anything resembling the yips, but from watching him, I do wonder whether his confidence has deteriorated to the point where instinctive reactions are being replaced by hesitation.

This has become a huge problem, and one Scott Parker needs to address immediately. As things stand, I would have more confidence in either Alphonse Areola or Finlay Herrick than a goalkeeper who currently appears unable to make the saves West Ham desperately need.

Areola is an experienced international goalkeeper whom Parker already knows from their time together at Fulham. I thought he deserved his opportunity against Blackburn, and nothing I witnessed yesterday has changed my mind.

Hermansen’s performance was among the biggest disappointments in our Blackburn player ratings, and continuing to select him simply because he was previously considered first choice doesn’t make much sense when his performances have deteriorated so badly.

Clearly, there is a bigger issue to address in January, when West Ham will have another opportunity to examine their goalkeeping options. But we’ve only just entered November, and there are still two months and a hell of a lot of fixtures to negotiate before the transfer window opens.

That’s an awful lot of potential points to lose while waiting for a goalkeeper to rediscover his confidence. Parker has an international break in which to make some important decisions, and I would suggest the identity of his first-choice goalkeeper should be very near the top of that list.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. #2124. Michael Olise Gives Chelsea A Hard Xabi Alonso Wide-Player Benchmark

Michael Olise has moved from former Chelsea academy footnote to genuine Ballon d’Or conversation piece.

That should land at Cobham as more than a wistful what-if.

Bavarian Football Works relayed comments from former Chelsea and France winger Florent Malouda, who described the Bayern Munich forward as a live Ballon d’Or contender if France turn their World Cup run into silverware.

The point is not simply that Chelsea once had Olise in the building. It is that his rise provides a hard benchmark for the type of final-third specialist Xabi Alonso must either develop or buy.

Chelsea have spent heavily on attacking upside. Olise’s rise is a reminder that the real separator is not potential, but repeatable elite production.

Bayern’s own season review underlines why the conversation has accelerated.

The German champions confirmed Olise finished 2025/26 with 25 goals and 28 assists from 57 competitive appearances. He was also named Bayern’s Player of the Season for a second straight year, finishing ahead of Harry Kane and Luis Diaz in the fan vote.

That is elite production from a wide creator who does not play like a chalk-on-boots winger.

Olise can receive wide, drift inside, manipulate the half-space and still deliver the last pass at match-winning speed. Chelsea have bought attackers with pieces of that profile, but few have joined the dots with that level of consistency.

There is an obvious recruitment lesson here.

The market will now price that blend of creativity, ball security and goal threat close to superstar territory. If Chelsea want an Alonso attack that controls games rather than simply runs through them, the next winger decision cannot be judged on pace or upside alone.

It has to be judged on game control, final-third calm and repeat output.

The World Cup has sharpened the comparison.

Chelsea’s official France preview noted that Malo Gusto was in the same squad as N’Golo Kante and former Blues academy player Olise. It was a neat reminder of how many Chelsea strands sit inside Didier Deschamps’ group.

Bundesliga’s tournament analysis was even more revealing. It noted that Olise had reached 10 France goal involvements in 18 appearances and was named Player of the Match on his World Cup debut against Senegal.

That same analysis highlighted the tactical adjustment that moved Olise closer to Kylian Mbappe after half-time. His passing range then became the lever that changed the game.

That matters for Chelsea because Alonso’s best Bayer Leverkusen sides were built around players who could receive between lines without killing the tempo. Cole Palmer already gives Chelsea one version of that.

The wider question is whether the squad has another attacker who can bend matches from the opposite side without turning every possession into a one-v-one.

Olise’s Chelsea connection can easily become sentimental. The sharper editorial point is structural.

This is the calibre of player who once passed through the academy pathway and later became a global-level attacker elsewhere. Chelsea cannot change that history, but they can use it to stress-test the next wave of decisions.

That matters for Tyrique George, Kendry Paez, Estevao and any new wide signing.

ReadChelsea has already looked at how Tyrique George’s likely Everton return gives Alonso a fresh winger audit. Olise’s current standing raises the standard of that discussion.

He is no longer just a former academy name or a missed Premier League target. He is evidence of what happens when technical security, role clarity and patience meet elite coaching.

For Alonso, that is the real value of the Olise noise.

Chelsea do not need to turn every academy exit into regret. They do need to recognise the standard now being set by one of Cobham’s former pupils.

The next wide forward who gets serious minutes at Stamford Bridge has to be assessed against that ceiling, not against the safer language of potential.

#2123. Arsenal handed major William Saliba injury boost ahead of Manchester City showdown

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have received a significant boost with William Saliba returning to first-team training. He’s spent almost four months out with a back injury.

The French international was seen returning to training on the pitch. Sparking hope that he could be back in action soon for Mikel Arteta.

Saliba has been sidelined since he suffered the injury in France’s 2-0 semi-final defeat to Spain. The defender was forced off after only 30 minutes after playing the full 90 in his previous five starts at the tournament.

The player was confirmed by Arsenal to be sidelined for a lengthy period of time in July. He did not require surgery, meaning he can begin working back into action.

His latest progress comes at an important point in Arsenal’s season, with several major fixtures approaching.

The 25-year-old has travelled to the Czech Republic with Arsenal’s squad to face Slavia Prague in the Champions League.

Arteta confirmed that Saliba had played for 45 minutes in a behind-closed-moors game last week as part of his rehab.

He then returned to full first team training on Tuesday, providing another defensive option for the club.

Arteta spoke ahead of the game against Slavia Prague. He said that Saliba is happy with his recovery and is at a point where he will be used if needed.

The Arsenal manager has not confirmed if the Frenchman will be involved tonight.

Arsenal have a busy schedule ahead after today’s Champions League game at Slavia Prague.

The defender played 50 times last season, helping Arsenal win the Premier League and reach the Champions League final. He also ranked 29th in the men’s Ballon d’Or in October.

His return to training is a major step in his road to full fitness, but it remains to be seen if he will play against Slavia Prague tonight.

#2122. Chelsea Could Claim Share of £164m Compensation

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Chelsea fans have followed Manchester City’s financial rules case for years. Everyone at Stamford Bridge knows a guilty verdict could cost City serious money, and much of it could end up with rivals lower down the table.

Reports now suggest an independent panel has reached a decision. If that holds up, the scale of any Chelsea Manchester City compensation payout could run into tens of millions of pounds for the Blues alone.

The Premier League charged Manchester City with 115 alleged breaches of financial regulations in February 2023. That followed a four-year investigation into the club’s accounts. The case has hung over the competition ever since. Every top-flight club knows the outcome could change how recent Premier League seasons are remembered.

This matters directly to Chelsea. If City are found to have broken spending and disclosure rules, clubs who finished below them could argue they lost out on prize money and European qualification. Chelsea have competed directly with City for top four places and cup competitions throughout that period.

According to The Athletic, Manchester City were found guilty on all but one of the 115 charges by the independent panel. The Premier League has not confirmed the verdict or issued a statement.

Manchester City have not accepted any suggestion of guilt. The club released a statement addressing the ongoing process directly.

“The Premier League process remains ongoing, with significant elements to be completed, and subject to strict confidentiality. As such, Manchester City FC’s position remains consistent with the Club’s statement of February 2023. ‘The Club has diligently respected due process for eight years on the basis that the Premier League Board and Executive would behave as an independent, impartial and fair-minded regulator, free from partisan influence.'”

That statement makes clear the club does not see the case as settled. Until the Premier League confirms a result, the reported verdict will linger on for official sentencing.

Data specialists at OLBG have estimated the potential Chelsea Manchester City compensation windfall if City are found guilty. They believe City could owe domestic rivals well over £500m in lost Premier League prize money if the charges are proven.

Within that total, London clubs are said to be due around £164m between them. Chelsea, Arsenal and Tottenham Hotspur are among the clubs who could be entitled to a share. Other Premier League sides could benefit too, depending on where they finished compared with City in the seasons in question.

Prize money in the Premier League is tied closely to league position. If City gained points unfairly, that would have pushed a rival down the table and cost them money too. That is the basis for the OLBG figures, and it explains why the sums involved are so large.

For Chelsea, even a share of that £164m pot would be a big boost. It could help fund new signings, or simply ease the pressure around the kind of transfer valuations at Stamford Bridge the club faces every window.

City chairman Khaldoon Al Mubarak has already made clear the case is far from finished. He described the process as having “a long way to run.” That points to appeals and further legal steps, whatever the panel has decided.

Chelsea, for their part, carry on with life under Xabi Alonso regardless of how the case unfolds. Attention stays on squad rotation and Xabi Alonso’s squad management strategy, not on any potential windfall. The financial case will not change anything on the pitch in the short term.

The Premier League has stayed silent on the reported verdict, and confidentiality remains strict around the process, according to reports from Football.London. Chelsea fans should expect a long wait before any compensation, if it comes at all, reaches the club’s accounts.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

#2121. Slavia Prague 1-2 Arsenal: Madueke and Gyokeres fire Gunners to Champions League victory

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal moved up to third in the Champions League table after beating Slavia Prague 2-1 at the Fortuna Arena.

Noni Madueke opened the scoring after nine minutes, finishing calmly after Viktor Gyokeres sent him through on goal.

The Swedish striker then doubled Arsenal’s advantage with an excellent finish in the 38th minute. However, a much stronger second-half performance from Slavia made for an uncomfortable finish.

Wiktor Nowak scored a late goal to put pressure on the Gunners. They held on to take all three points.

Mikel Arteta made six changes from the Liverpool win on Sunday. William Saliba returns to the squad after his back injury.

The defender was left on the bench, with Ezri Konsa playing alongside Gabriel Magalhães in central defence.

Arsenal started brightly and took control of the game when they won the long ball forward. Gyokeres beat his man and found Madueke with a clever through ball.

The winger used his pace to get into the penalty area before sliding his finish through goalkeeper Nazar Domchak’s legs.

Arsenal continued to create chances, with Mikel Merino going close on several occasions. Domchak also produced an important save to deny the midfielder from close range.

Merino eventually helped Arsenal extend their lead. After winning possession in the Slavia half, Declan Rice found Gyokeres, who exchanged passes with the Spaniard before curling a superb finish into the far corner.

The visitors could have added a third before the break. Gabriel’s header from a corner was cleared off the line.

Arsenal appeared to be in control at half-time.

But Slavia upped the tempo and started to create more space, forcing Arsenal into a defensive stance. Early on, Oskar Kubiak fired a long-range shot, forcing David Raya to push it away.

Arteta got increasingly frustrated as Arsenal struggled to hold on to the ball and dominate the midfield.

They tried to keep pressing, but Arsenal struggled to create much at the other end. Madueke did get into a good position to shoot from an angle but didn’t have enough power to cause any problems for Domchak.

Slavia were denied a goal when Gabriel headed the ball away from the goal line. Raya made a brilliant stop to keep the shot out while the penalty area was crowded.

The Arsenal keeper made another stunning save as Kubiak powered a header from a cross. Raya deflected the ball onto the post to keep his team ahead by two goals.

Slavia scored their breakthrough goal in the 90th minute after David Jurasek played a low cross from the left. Nowak was able to run into the box to score past Raya.

It created a nervy few minutes of added time as Arsenal had to defend their lead.

Slavia could find no way back into the game as Stepan Chaloupek headed wide and Mikulas Konecny threatened in the dying moments, but his effort lacked confidence as Raya made light work of saving.

Arsenal secured their third win in their last four Champions League games to move to 10 points and into third place in the group stage.

The Gunners have now gone unbeaten in their last ten Champions League away games to set a new club record.

However, Arteta will be looking for a much better display from his side when they resume action in the Premier League against Hull City on Saturday.

#2120. Parker’s words prove he has so much more to offer than his predecessor | West Ham News

West Ham fans may have voiced their fury as the side slid one place further down the table after a poor run of results, but at least in Scott Parker the Hammers have a manager who understands how it really, really feels to have to witness that dross.

Unlike his predecessor Nuno Espírito Santo, Parker seems to have the ability to connect with the fan base and offer real empathy – along with hope that he will at least, work his nuts off to see a turnaround.

Nuno Espirito Santo was reduced to the same platitudes, the same apologies, but with the sense that it was just for the cameras and that he’d shrug his shoulders as soon as the interview was over, leaving someone else to fix the chaos in the dressing room and on the training pitch. West Ham’s training videos usually showed an assistant taking training, with Nuno either passive or absent entirely.

Even after a year Nuno struggled to convince fans that his ‘concern’ was heartfelt.

Parker has already surpassed his predecessor in terms of being ‘present’. Both his pronouncements and his actions – such as his first-game, half time team talk demonstrate his ability to wear his heart on his sleeve.

His words reported yesterday by footballleagueworld.co.uk do suggest, unlike his predecessor, that he’s genuinely hurting, along with the rest of us:

“Sounding somewhat hoarse after his team’s 2-1 defeat at Blackburn, Scott Parker spoke after the matchabout his disappointment at the result. Parker said that he’d talked about his team’s need to get a result from this match, even if it wasn’t playing the sort of football that he want his team to play.

He had sympathy for West Ham supporters, saying that he “wanted to get three points” for them, adding that, “What I can say is that I’m fully committed here, and I understand the pain, I understand where the fan base are.”

Parker added that he understands that the unhappiness of fans isn’t entirely a response to recent performances alone, saying that, “That’s not just this season, it’s come over a little bit of time.”

And he admitted that he was also hurting from this result: “I think everyone’s hurting here, and tonight I’m hurting because I understand that the only way we can change things is winning”

And it matters. In these dark days for the football club, mid table in the second division, leadership is what will unite team and supporters to operate together and hopefully pull the club out of its slide. Leadership doesn’t always yell, ( he’s done that too), sometimes it merely shows understanding and empathy whilst demanding better.

#2119. Iraola admits uncertainty over Liverpool player’s international involvement after injury scare

Andoni Iraola has expressed concern as to whether one Liverpool player will link up with his country during this month’s international break.

The Reds go into the brief pause to the season off the back of a 3-1 victory at Crystal Palace, with some of the gloss from that result removed by a questionable stoppage-time penalty for the home side and an injury scare to Dominik Szoboszlai.

The 26-year-old pulled up towards the end of the first half at Selhurst Park on Sunday and played with a knee brace after the interval until he was substituted for Trey Nyoni in the 69th minute.

After the match, Iraola admitted that the Hungarian wasn’t operating at 100% yesterday, having only recently returned from a thigh issue.

The Basque said (via liverpoolfc.com): “I don’t know [if it’s] a problem, but he definitely wasn’t 100%. Let’s see. The last thing we need now is to lose players in the international games.

“Last time, in the international break, we lost three very important players, so now I’m praying – it’s the last thing we need.

“I think he extended a little bit the knee. It was fine, but not 100%, so he has to be careful to see if he can play with the national team or not. We will see, and also hope that it doesn’t happen like the other break where we lost Alex [Isak], Cody [Gakpo] and Dom. Touch wood we are in a better place in two weeks.”

On whether Szoboszlai would link up with the Hungary squad for their upcoming UEFA Nations League fixtures, Iraola said: “I know he will do everything to go because he loves to play with the national team, but he was not 100% when he finished the game. I suppose they will have to analyse, the medical staff, and see what it is.

“I know what I want, but I also have to understand the national teams. I’m sure that Dom, if he is fine he will want to go, but I’m not sure if he’s going to be fine.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

Along with Iraola, Liverpool fans with no vested interest in the Hungary national team will surely be hoping that Szoboszlai rests up during the international window rather than representing his country.

Marco Rossi’s side are away to Georgia next Saturday and at home to Northern Ireland three days later, with promotion to League A well within their grasp heading into that double header.

As captain of his country, the midfielder will no doubt be striving to play his part over the coming week or so, but there must also be a duty towards the club which pays his wages and given him a platform to reach an even higher level in his career.

Liverpool resume their season in 13 days’ time with an Anfield grudge match against Manchester United, and they still have 11 more matches to play before the end of December, so managing players’ fitness will be vital during that relentless period.

Szoboszlai has been a frustrating figure at times this term, with debate raging as to where exactly his best position is on the pitch, but his creativity came to the fore in the win over Palace on Sunday.

Also, as he’s shown on several occasions, he possesses that X-factor to conjure up show-stopping and often result-altering goals.

Whatever decisions might be taken between Liverpool and Hungary, fingers crossed that our vice-captain is back to 100% for the Man United clash on the 22nd of this month.

#2118. Jamie Gittens suffers Chelsea injury blow with England U21s

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Gittens has withdrawn from the England Under-21 squad. He suffered an ankle injury during Tuesday’s qualifier against Kazakhstan at Molineux. The Chelsea winger left the ground on crutches. He has returned to Stamford Bridge, where the Jamie Gittens injury will now be assessed by the club’s medical staff.

Gittens picked up the knock after a challenge during the second half of England’s win over Kazakhstan. He was withdrawn as a precaution. He did not take any further part in the match. England have been careful not to overstate the issue and no further reports have come out further.

The 21-year-old struggled to put weight on the affected ankle and was seen limping around Molineux after full time. That prompted an immediate release back to his club, rather than any attempt to assess him further in camp.

England Under-21 boss Lee Carsley addressed the injury directly after the game. He confirmed the nature of the knock and the decision to send Gittens home.

“Jamie got a kick on his ankle,” Carsley said after the match. “We never take any risks. With the age of these players, we don’t ever keep them in camp to see how they are. He will go back.”

England’s statement on the squad changes, detailed in a report from Football.London, confirmed the wider fallout from the fixture.

“Mukasa’s call-up follows the withdrawal of Chelsea’s Jamie Gittens and Norwich City pair Kellen Fisher and Ben Chrisene through injury,” the statement read. West Ham midfielder Divine Mukasa has been called up as cover for all three.

Gittens has been an unused substitute in every Premier League fixture Chelsea have played this season, with his only two appearances under Xabi Alonso coming in the Carabao Cup. Those minutes have offered him little platform after his big summer move.

Alonso’s squad selection has been a talking point since he took charge. Our earlier look at Xabi Alonso’s squad choices at Stamford Bridge covered how competitive things have become. An ankle injury will not change his pecking order in the short term. It does, though, remove any chance of him pushing his case this week.

Chelsea’s break has brought other headlines too. Cole Palmer also withdrew, for entirely different reasons, after his recall by senior boss Thomas Tuchel to the full England squad. Chelsea will be watching both returns closely before domestic football resumes.

Chelsea have not yet issued a formal update on the severity of the injury. Any recovery timeline will depend on an assessment by the club’s medical team once Gittens is back at Cobham. Nothing beyond the crutches seen at Molineux has been confirmed.

Given his limited game time this season, the immediate concern is less about missed fixtures than a setback to his integration under Alonso. Fans can follow how the squad shapes up before the next fixtures through our recent line-up updates. Further news on Gittens is expected once Chelsea’s medical staff finish their evaluation.

#2117. Arsenal injury latest: Konsa concern, Saliba return and Saka update

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal will hope Ezri Konsa recovers in time for Saturday’s 3pm Premier League clash with Hull City after he came off during their Champions League win over Slavia Prague.The return of William Saliba to the matchday squad is a great boost for Mikel Arteta ahead of the game, but Bukayo Saka remains out as he continues to recover from his quad injury.

Here is all the latest Arsenal injury news and when the three players are expected to return.

Konsa started Arsenal’s 2-1 victory over Slavia Prague on Wednesday but came off in the second half after experiencing discomfort.

He appeared to limp during the first half, so Arsenal will monitor his fitness closely ahead of Saturday’s match.

Arteta said the change was not a tactical one, as Konsa had been playing a lot of games recently and had some problems during the match.

The Arsenal boss is likely to give an update ahead of Saturday’s game depending on how he recovers in the coming days.

Konsa’s absence will leave Arsenal without as many reliable centre backs. However, Gabriel will play a key role in the backline, and Arteta has other options in Cristhian Mosquera and Piero Hincapie.

Potential return date: November 7 vs Hull City (3pm kick-off).

A positive from the Gunners’ trip to Prague was the inclusion of Saliba in the squad.

The defender has not played this season after picking up a long-term back injury. The issue got worse during his time with France at the World Cup finals, but he managed to avoid surgery.

Saliba has steadily built up his workload and played for 45 minutes in a behind-closed-moors game. He returned to full training on Tuesday and travelled with the squad to take on Slavia Prague.

Although he did not come off the bench, his inclusion shows he is getting close to being back in contention.

Arteta said that Saliba was feeling confident, but it will depend on the needs of the squad.

It gives Arsenal an extra experienced centre-back, and if Konsa is not available against Hull, then he could make an appearance on the pitch.

Potential return date: November 7 vs Hull City (3pm kick-off).

Saka is currently the main attacking concern for Arsenal after he has been sidelined with a quad injury for the last four games.

The winger suffered the injury approximately 10 minutes into the Arsenal Champions League clash against Bayern Munich last month.

The original fears were that Saka would be out for up to eight weeks. Arsenal have not given any confirmation on how long it will take him to make a full recovery.

He will need to undergo another scan to get a better idea of the amount of swelling and fluid in the area. The medical staff will also want to see if the tendon is involved.

This means it is unlikely he plays against Hull. Arsenal are not expected to bring him back too quickly ahead of the international break.

Arteta will miss one of his most influential attackers. Hopefully the next scan will give a clearer indication of how long he is likely to be out for.

Expected return date: Unknown. Saka is not expected to face Hull City on November 7.

Arsenal host Hull City at the Emirates Stadium on Saturday, November 7th.

The game comes ahead of a tough sequence of games which sees Manchester City, Real Madrid and Tottenham on the horizon. Arteta will want as many defensive and attacking threats on hand.
