#2116. Manu Koné: Roma slap £86m price tag on Chelsea target

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Roma have put an £86m (€100m) price tag on Manu Koné, according to Gazzetta dello Sport, reviving the Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link that first emerged over the summer. Roma want any interested club, including Chelsea, to know the price before serious talks can even begin.

Koné remains under contract at the Stadio Olimpico, and he has become a key part of Roma’s midfield since his move from Borussia Mönchengladbach. The Gazzetta report reads like a warning to potential buyers rather than proof of any talks between the two clubs.

Roma sporting director Tony D’Amico has confirmed Chelsea made contact late in the summer window, though he was quick to draw a clear line under how far things went.

“There was an inquiry and some preliminary contact with Chelsea on the final day of the transfer window. No official offers arrived that would allow us to approach the player. Something was imagined that simply didn’t happen. There was no official offer; the final day of the transfer window got everyone a little worked up.”

D’Amico’s comments confirm Chelsea looked at signing Koné before the deadline, but they stop well short of showing an official bid was ever made. He also explained why Roma had no wish to sell a key player so close to the window closing.

“Before the match against Fiorentina (on 24 August), I said it would be difficult for us to deprive ourselves of important players so near to the end of the transfer session, and so it proved. Kone is an important player, we’re happy he stayed with us, and we’re confident he’ll give his contribution during the season.”

Chelsea’s midfield has changed shape under Xabi Alonso since the summer, with Morgan Rogers arriving to add depth and competition for places. That signing came during a window that also saw exits acceleration under Xabi Alonso, as the head coach reshaped his squad around his own style.

Koné’s ball winning and his range of progressive passing would suit Alonso well, should Chelsea ever revisit their interest. Supporters have already seen how Alonso rotates his midfield for the biggest games, including the recent derby line-up selections against Brentford.

For now, Rogers and Chelsea’s existing central options give Alonso enough cover without needing an immediate signing. Roma’s reluctance to sell also means any deal would need patience rather than a quick resolution.

Nothing in D’Amico’s comments suggests Roma are open to a sale in the near term, and Koné has given no sign that he wants to leave. The £86m valuation, reported by Football London, stands as Roma’s marker rather than an asking price involved with Chelsea.

Will Chelsea return with a more formal approach in a future window? D’Amico’s comments give no hint either way. The Manu Koné Chelsea transfer link is unlikely to fade given Chelsea’s history of scouting France’s leading midfielders, but any fresh move will have to wait for Roma’s stance to soften.

#2114. Arsenal vs Hull City preview: Gunners face draw specialists at the Emirates

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have the opportunity to strengthen their position in the Premier League table when they welcome Hull City to the Emirates Stadium on Saturday.

Mikel Arteta’s side secured a three-point lead after their 1-0 victory over Liverpool at Anfield and then followed it up with a 2-1 win in the Champions League against Slavia Prague on Wednesday.

The Gunners have won four games in a row but are looking to stretch their lead to six points ahead of Manchester City’s clash against Nottingham Forest later on Saturday. They are undefeated at home this season.

However, Hull will be looking to cause problems in North London.

Sergej Jakirovic’s side sit 11th after collecting 11 points from their opening nine league matches.

They have drawn five times, sharing the highest number of stalemates in the division. Three of their last four league fixtures have also ended level.

They drew 2-2 with Ipswich Town in their last game, but they needed to get back on track after falling behind. Recently they have failed to convert good performances into wins.

Despite Arsenal expecting to dominate possession, Hull’s 5-man defence may cause them problems.

The visitors have also had defensive issues recently, as they have failed to keep a clean sheet in their last seven games in all competitions. This may lead to chances being created for the Gunners attackers.

Arteta has to deal with his squad after the midweek trip to Prague. William Saliba is back in contention after recovering from his back injury but Cristhian Mosquera may stay in the squad if he is not rushed back in.

Ezri Konsa is a doubt after being withdrawn from Wednesday’s game with a knee problem. Bukayo Saka is also still sidelined as is Max Dowman.

Hull will be missing Matt Crooks who is suspended. Eliot Matazo, Matt Targett, Darko Gyabi, Ilyas Ansah and Charlie Hughes are also expected to miss out.

Raya; Timber, Mosquera, Gabriel, Calafiori; Guimarães, Rice; Madueke, Ødegaard, Tzolis; Havertz.

Tzolakis; Coyle, Ajayi, Egan, Mendy, Giles; Belloumi, Iroegbunam, Slater, Stroud; McBurnie.

Arsenal will face Hull City at the Emirates Stadium on Saturday, November 7. Kick off is at 3pm GMT.

Despite the fact that Hull has a tendency to draw, Arsenal remains a strong favorite to win this match. Arteta’s team will want to earn as many points as possible ahead of the international break.

Despite not being called upon this season, West Ham’s second choice ‘keeper and many people’s must-start for the Blackburn game in which Mads Hermansen fared so poorly, was not even available for the Blackburn tie, according to reports this afternoon.

Which will be concerning for Scott Parker as well as for supporters who have seen the the Hammers’ first choice goal keeper exhibit the same weaknesses, exposed again and again as the Hammers conceded eleven times in four games without Hermansen so much as laying a glove on any of the goal-bound efforts.

One out of form, the other injured: Goal keeper crisis looming…

Hammers.news claim that reserve keeper Areola actually had to withdraw from the squad entirely during the warm up at Ewood Park – with an as yet unspecified injury.

There’ll be huge consternation behind the scenes: Without knowing the exact cause of Areola’s problem, the 6’5″ French shot stopper has suffered from back trouble in the past which has somewhat blighted his domestic and  international career.

With only England u20 keeper Finn Herrick as their third option, something of a crisis looms, dependent entirely upon Areola’s ability to recover during the two weeks that lie ahead.

#2112. Joao Pedro Injury: Chelsea Forward Misses Brentford Defeat

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Joao Pedro missed Chelsea’s 3-0 Premier League defeat to Brentford with a knee problem, and the injury has since ruled him out of Brazil’s squad for the friendlies against Australia and India.

He was absent from Xabi Alonso’s confirmed lineups against Brentford, with Chelsea turning to other forward options instead.

Brazil’s coaching staff confirmed the withdrawal, pointing to the same knee issue that kept him out of the derby. Chelsea have not set a firm return date for Joao Pedro, leaving supporters to wait for further updates during the international break.

The Joao Pedro injury situation took a new turn when the Standard reported that he is seeing a specialist during the international break to speed up his recovery.

According to the Standard, the forward is using this pause in the calendar to get ahead of the problem rather than wait for Chelsea to resume domestic action.

Chelsea have not issued their own statement on the specialist visit, and the club’s official line on Joao Pedro’s fitness remains limited to his withdrawal from matchday duty.

Full detail on the reported treatment can be found in football.london’s reported injury update, which first carried the specialist claim alongside Xabi Alonso’s comments on the striker’s season.

Until Chelsea confirm otherwise, the Standard’s report stands as the clearest picture of how the club are handling the knee concern.

It suggests a measured approach, with recovery prioritised over rushing Joao Pedro back into contention for the next fixture.

Before the injury forced him out, Xabi Alonso had made his admiration for Joao Pedro clear. The Chelsea manager ranked the forward among the Premier League’s elite strikers, alongside Manchester City’s Erling Haaland.

“If you count how many top strikers we have in the Premier League, I’m sure that João is there, Haaland for sure is there.

“So I would rate him that way. He has a very strong goal this year: to have a great season, to score many goals, and to be a nominee for not just this month but more months. I am not surprised, but really happy with the kind of player that we have here with João.”

With Joao Pedro sidelined, Alonso must lean again on the depth built into his squad.

His approach to rotation, part of the squad management this season, faces another test while his in-form forward is unavailable.

#2111. Thomas Tuchel makes Noni Madueke decision as five Arsenal stars earn England call-ups

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Noni Madueke has missed another England opportunity, despite making an encouraging impression during his recent run in the Arsenal starting line-up.

Thomas Tuchel has named his 25-man squad for the Nations League fixtures against Croatia and Spain in November, but Arsenal winger Madueke misses out again after failing to make the cut following a missed international camp.

He has been left out despite making the most of Bukayo Saka’s absence to increase his involvement under Mikel Arteta.

Madueke scored in the recent Premier League win against Nottingham Forest and then again in Wednesday’s Champions League win against Slavia Prague.

He had a legitimate claim to be named in the squad, and Tuchel admitted that Madueke was very close to being included.

The England boss acknowledged that Madueke has benefitted from his extra games at Arsenal due to Saka’s injury.

But Tuchel chose to keep the same front three that he had during the last international camp. The German wants to reward those players who have earned their place in the team and not make changes every time they are playing well at club level.

This means Madueke will be left frustrated, having to wait until next time.

Madueke has played under Tuchel before, so it shows how much he thinks of him as a player. He featured in England’s 2026 World Cup campaign, so it is noticeable that he was left out this time.

Although he can’t control the battle for places, he will now be able to continue building his case. With Saka still injured, he will have the chance to play more regularly and solidify his place in Arteta’s plans.

However, Madueke is not the only player at Arsenal who has had positive news.

Declan Rice, Eberechi Eze, Ezri Konsa and Myles Lewis-Skelly have all earned or retained their spots in Tuchel’s squad. Ben White also comes back into the England squad, having missed the last camp.

It is another chance for White to re-establish himself on the world stage after his knee injury kept him out of the World Cup.

It also means Arsenal have five players in the squad after Saka was left out due to injury.

England play Croatia at Wembley on November 12 before heading to Spain on November 15. Madueke will be watching from home, knowing he was almost part of the squad.

Now he needs to build on his recent form and become a consistent performer for Arsenal. Whilst Tuchel has left the door open, Madueke needs to give the England boss another reason to change his mind.

#2110. Pep Guardiola Speaks Out as Man City Found Guilty of 114 Charges

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester City have been found guilty of 114 financial charges, the Premier League has confirmed. The verdict follows an eight-week hearing that opened almost two years ago. It closes one of the most significant disciplinary cases in the competition’s history.

Former head coach Pep Guardiola has already broken his silence. Reaction to the Pep Guardiola Man City charges verdict is spreading fast across the division, with Chelsea among the clubs watching closely.

Guardiola joined Manchester City in 2016 and turned them into the dominant force in English football. He left his post earlier this summer, with the club having already worked through plans to replace Pep Guardiola at City.

His exit means he holds no formal role at the Etihad. Yet he moved quickly to make his loyalty clear once the ruling landed. According to the football.london report that first carried his statement, Guardiola posted directly to his supporters.

“I want every single @ManCity fan to know I am here; more than ever. I am, always have been, always will, be behind my club, my owner, my chairman [Khaldoon Al Mubarak], Ferran [Soriano], the players, staff, Enzo [Maresca] and you, our unique supporters. Let me be clear: we will get through this together. I love you all.”

Manchester City chief executive Ferran Soriano has confirmed that the club intends to appeal the verdict. The statement from Guardiola comes to City’s defence despite him leaving the role this summer. He is still viewed as the man who built this era of success, even though he no longer sits in the dugout at the Etihad.

The Pep Guardiola Man City charges case matters just as much to Chelsea as it does to Manchester City themselves. Chelsea, along with Arsenal and Tottenham, are among the Premier League clubs directly affected by the outcome. Every point City may have gained through breaches has a knock on effect further down the table.

Most top flight clubs including Chelsea are now pushing for City to face relegation as a consequence of the verdict. Some are reported to be weighing up legal action of their own. Guardiola has spoken about Chelsea before, including Guardiola’s previous comments on Chelsea. This latest statement fits a pattern of public backing for Manchester City, despite the criticism now directed at the club.

For Chelsea supporters, the case is not just about points on a table. It touches on the integrity of seasons they competed in directly. That is why so many rivals are refusing to let the matter rest with a guilty verdict alone.

Manchester City’s appeal process is now the next stage in a case that has already run for nearly two years. Ferran Soriano has made clear the club will contest the findings. No date has been confirmed for when that appeal will be heard.

Until then, no points deductions, penalties or changes to past titles have been confirmed by the Premier League. Chelsea and their rivals will be watching the appeal timeline just as closely as they watched the original hearing. The final outcome could still be a long way off.

Alisson Becker’s outstanding performance in Liverpool’s 3-1 win away to Crystal Palace on Sunday was reflected in what one Eagles player told Alexis Mac Allister.

The Reds’ number 1 had shipped criticism in recent days for errors in the defeats to Arsenal and Fenerbahce, but he was back to his world-class best at Selhurst Park yesterday as he turned in a man-of-the-match display for his side.

The Brazilian pulled off six saves in total against Pierre Sage’s team, including a stunning reaction stop to keep out a fearsome volley from Daichi Kamada just a couple of minutes before the Argentine scored the visitors’ opening goal.

Speaking to BBC Sport after the match (via liverpoolfc.com), Mac Allister was singing Alisson’s praises and shared what the Japan international had told him about the Liverpool goalkeeper.

The Reds’ number 10 said of the Brazil stopper: “He’s so good, especially the save from Kamada’s shot [in the first half]. Kamada couldn’t believe the save he made and he was telling me, but that’s who he is.

“He is an amazing goalkeeper and an amazing guy as well. I’m really, really happy for him.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

Alisson had endured some unusually shaky moments in recent weeks, but he reminded us all on Sunday that class is permanent and that he remains pivotal to Liverpool’s hopes of success this season.

Such was Alisson’s performance at Selhurst Park that the Premier League’s official X profile posted a compilation of his saves from the win over the south London outfit, with the goalkeeper treating us to his own showreel.

Anyone who might’ve thought that the Brazil international was in irreversible decline was made to think again yesterday by a masterclass from Liverpool’s legendary number 1.

#2106. Arsenal transfer interest faces major obstacle as Santos secure £83m teenage talent

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal may have to rethink their interest in Santos forward Ignacio Pereira after the Brazilian club secured the teenager’s future with a substantial release clause.

The 16-year-old has caught the eye of a number of European clubs after an impressive campaign in Santos’ youth teams. The likes of Arsenal are said to have been watching his progress.

However, Santos have acted swiftly to secure the services of one of their most exciting young talents. Pereira signed his first professional contract with the club on November 4, tying him to the club until December 2029.

The deal also includes a 100 million euro release clause (around £83.4 million), according to Brazilian journalist Bruno Lima from UOL.

This poses a major problem for Arsenal if they are looking to sign the Argentine but also offers a lot of security for Santos, as they want to develop him as a first-team player.

Pereira has signed a new deal amid increasing interest in his development. Reports in Brazil have previously said that Arsenal, Real Betis, AC Milan, Lille, Standard Liège and Zenit are all keeping an eye on the rising star.

The club have now secured his services for another three years. The new agreement was awaiting registration with the Brazilian Football Confederation when the details emerged.

Pereira has spent three years at Santos, having joined from Juventude in 2023. He previously developed at Grêmio before moving to the São Paulo club.

Pereira has certainly lived up to the expectations. He scored an impressive 6 goals in 17 games in the Brazilian Under-17 Championship, making him the second-highest scorer for Santos. He also scored 4 goals in the Paulista Under-17 Championship.

This is very promising. It is his first season playing at under-17 level.

Pereira has been compared to Julián Álvarez due to his mobility and ability to play outside the box.

But the youngster cites another big name up front as his main inspiration. According to UOL, he cites Manchester City’s Erling Haaland as his inspiration, while Lionel Messi is still his football idol.

His profile suggests a versatile number nine who can play lower down the pitch, be an attacking midfielder and play with another striker. He has impressed the youth coaches at Santos with his finishing ability, physicality and desire to win.

He has a long way to go. Reports from Brazil suggest that Santos want to improve his tactical awareness and behaviour before making him more responsible.

He is being considered by the club for inclusion in their squad for the 2027 Copa São Paulo de Futebol Júnior (known as Copinha) and could start on the bench.

This interest from Arsenal does not necessarily mean that there is an imminent move for Pereira. The player is only 16, and a potential deal is made much more complicated due to a new contract.

Maybe the best course of action for Mikel Arteta’s recruitment team is to keep an eye on him. Santos has made it clear where they stand, and Pereira can now work towards his dream. Being in their first team.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bournemouth will block any Alex Scott Chelsea transfer in January. That is according to reports from the Daily Mail and BBC Sport. Both outlets say the Cherries want to keep their young midfielder as they fight to protect their Premier League status this season.

Chelsea have been linked with serious interest in the 22-year-old. Reports suggest the Blues have already tested Bournemouth’s resolve with a bid worth close to £64 million. Bournemouth have given no indication they are willing to negotiate. Scott, the reports say, simply is not for sale this winter.

Reports in the Daily Mail and on BBC Sport state that Bournemouth have no intention of sanctioning a sale this winter. The club’s hierarchy reportedly view Scott as central to their survival plan, wary of leaning on new signings mid-season rather than trusting the squad they already have.

A fee of around £64 million, first reported by Football London, is said to have been offered by Chelsea during recent discussions.

Scott joined Bournemouth from Bristol City in 2023. He has quickly become one of the most sought-after young midfielders in English football. Interest from a club the size of Chelsea shows how far his reputation has grown, though Bournemouth’s stance makes a January move increasingly unlikely.

Scott’s rise has been underlined by his first appearance for England. He came off the bench for a 20-minute cameo as Thomas Tuchel’s side beat Czechia 2-0 in the Nations League. The manager praised him afterwards.

“Alex Scott has maybe one of the best performances in midfield in the Premier League since the start with Bournemouth.”

“Individually he’s one of the outstanding midfielders so he clearly deserves to be with us.”

“He took another step into the right direction. Very impressive and happy that he is on that route. Now he plays in Europe for Bournemouth and deserves to be with us.”

That praise from their former manager will only increase Chelsea’s interest. Scott’s energy and composure suit the identity Xabi Alonso wants to build at Stamford Bridge. The Spaniard has already shown he is willing to rotate his squad, as seen in Xabi Alonso’s squad management decisions this season.

Chelsea travel to face Bournemouth in the Premier League soon. It is a fixture that will give Alonso a close look at a player repeatedly linked with his club. Build-up to that game is already underway, with team news arriving ahead of the Bournemouth clash.

The prospects of an Alex Scott Chelsea transfer happening in January now look slim. Supporters hoping for fresh competition in central midfield may need patience, with Chelsea more likely to revisit their interest when the summer window opens.

It means he was not selected by Zinedine Zidane for France’s Nations League games against Italy and Turkey. This gives Arsenal a chance to continue working on his recovery. He will not be travelling for international games or playing in competitive matches.

Saliba has not featured since he suffered a back injury during France’s World Cup semi-final against Spain in July. Arteta has had to find alternatives in central defence in the early stages of the campaign.

Since transferring to Arsenal from Aston Villa in August, Konsa has played a part in filling the gap left by Saliba. His presence in the centre of defence complements Gabriel Magalhães and gives Arteta more options.

The England defender is also expected to be fit for the match against Hull City on Saturday. According to Arteta, the player’s replacement in the game against Slavia Prague was just a precaution after he reported some discomfort.

The fact that he has made a recovery means that Arsenal have the option to control how Saliba returns. Arteta has the possibility of bringing Saliba back gradually, as Konsa is ready to play and Mosquera is also available.

But Saliba’s return still matters. He needs to get back in shape before the fixtures start getting tougher after the international break.

Arteta has made it clear he will respect whatever decision Zidane makes regarding Saliba’s availability but acknowledges that he needs some game time after being out for so long.

France will play Italy on November 12 and then Turkey three days later. This means Saliba will be missing those matches, as Zidane named his 23-man squad on Friday.

This means he will be able to continue to be monitored by the club at their London Colney training ground. He won’t be able to return to play in international games ahead of his first appearance for the club.

Arsenal will be hoping that he can use the extra time to get himself ready for the next set of games, including a trip to Newcastle before playing Borussia Dortmund and Manchester City at home.

Arteta will be keen to have Saliba back in contention but will be focused on getting him back at the right time.

There is some immediate optimism on Konsa’s fitness for the game against Hull. But more importantly, Saliba has now progressed to a point where he can be included in the planning process.

#2102. Scott Parker Has a Selection Dilemma and the Numbers Are Damning

Plenty has already been written about Mads Hermansen, with transfer speculation continuing to swirl. Reports suggest Newcastle’s Nick Pope could be a target when the January window opens, but for now, West Ham’s goalkeeping situation remains firmly under the spotlight.

The numbers are difficult to ignore. A goalkeeper who isn’t making enough saves and is shipping goals at an alarming rate, 23 conceded in 15 games, is becoming a problem that cannot be overlooked.

One possible solution could be Alphonse Areola, who previously worked under Scott Parker at Fulham. There is also a potential opportunity for highly-rated youngster Finlay Herrick, who may be worth a closer look.

While Areola’s statistics last season hardly inspire confidence, conceding 37 goals in 20 appearances without recording a clean sheet, there is at least some context. By comparison, Hermansen managed seven clean sheets while conceding 28 goals in 18 matches.

The pair offer very different strengths and weaknesses! Areola is arguably the better shot-stopper but struggles when asked to play out from the back. Hermansen, meanwhile, has been preferred for his distribution and comfort in possession, yet questions remain over his save percentage and ability to keep the ball out of the net when it matters most.

Ex-Goalie Nuno failed to get the best out of Hermansen

Another concern is the absence of an established senior goalkeeping coach. That said, it’s worth remembering that the club’s former manager was a goalkeeper himself, while Rui Barbosa brought years of coaching and playing experience. Neither appeared able to significantly influence Hermansen’s development.

Currently, Billy Lepine, a former Dagenham & Redbridge youth player who has worked within the academy setup since 2008, occupies the role. Lukasz Fabianski is also assisting on a part-time basis with the academy.

And perhaps that’s where the story takes an even stranger twist.

Cast your mind back to 4th December 2024, when West Ham travelled to the King Power Stadium. Leicester won 3-1, ending a six-game winless run. On that night, West Ham peppered the Leicester goal with 31 shots, the highest number they had ever recorded in a Premier League match. Ten were on target and they dominated possession with 61%.

Standing between the posts for Leicester that evening?

None other than Mads Hermansen, who produced a performance worthy of Middle-earth, adopting Gandalf’s famous stance of “You shall not pass!” as he admirably defended his goal.

The previous year in the Championship he featured in the EFL team of the season. One of the most proactive ball playing keepers in the league, and completed 13 clean sheets.

If ever there was evidence of the “West Ham goalkeeper curse”, that might just be it.

The solution feels fairly straightforward. Bring Fabianski into the senior goalkeeping coaching setup, get Areola focused solely on football rather than the DJ decks, and bring in an experienced goalkeeper on loan in January to provide competition.

The ball is now firmly in Scott Parker’s court.

I have been a season ticket holder since the late eighties, so experienced the highs and lows of being a West Ham supporter. I previously wrote for OLAS and have contributed to a number of football publications in the past.

Middle of the park a bigger issue than front and back of the team

A top goalkeeper and a striker should be our priority in January

Now I am not saying Hermansen shouldn’t be relieved of his duties — he should be replaced.

However, last season’s Stats shout out what problems our Goalkeepers face ….. Areloa played his matches with Dinos and Dumbo, whoops sorry, Kilman in front if him, whilst tge majority of Hermansen’s matches had Dinos and Disasi.

Replace Kilman and whichever goalkeeper is in between the Hammers sticjs, will have improved Stats

Does Fabianski still have his boots and gloves? Seriously Herrick is much better at playing out from the back than the other two, he just seems lacking in height for me. Would he dominate his area in a way we have been calling out for this season?

Our “goalkeeper curse” is nothing compared to our “striker curse”.

#2099. Arsenal transfer news: Arsenal set January transfer priorities as Fabrizio Romano delivers Piero Hincapié update

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

In Arsenal transfer news, Arsenal could approach the January transfer window with a different focus after Fabrizio Romano revealed that Mikel Arteta has no intention of weakening his defensive options.

Piero Hincapié has been linked with a move to Barcelona. There have been reports that a deal could be done involving Alejandro Balde, but Romano expects the Ecuadorian to stay at Emirates Stadium.

Arsenal have been strengthening their defensive department under Arteta. Hincapié only made the switch permanently from Bayer Leverkusen in the summer after joining on loan.

Despite competition, the 24-year-old is a key player in Arteta’s plans. His versatility to play in either a centre back or left back role gives Arteta a valuable option.

However, we can also gain insight into where Arsenal should target their recruitment from Romano’s latest comments. Instead of changing their defensive options, it could be an opportunity to improve their attacking options.

However, we can also gain insight into where Arsenal should target their recruitment from Romano’s latest comments. Instead of changing their defensive options, it could be an opportunity to improve their attacking options.

However, Arsenal have little incentive to let him leave halfway through the season, as losing a two-in-one defender will cause problems for Arteta, given how much they need to do in multiple competitions.

Romano’s comments suggest that Arsenal are happy with their existing defensive unit and don’t want to cause any disruption to their balance. He also suggested that Hincapié is an important player for them, meaning he’s unlikely to leave in January.

That leaves Barcelona needing to explore alternative targets if they want to strengthen their backline. A deal involving Balde and Hincapié does not currently appear to be a realistic option.

The more interesting part of Romano’s report is Arsenal’s potential business at the other end of the field.

He feels they will be looking to make a move if the right attacking player comes up for grabs. But he doesn’t think they will make a signing just because they can in January.

This highlights the issues clubs face with mid-season transfers. Often selling clubs will be unwilling to lose key players, and finding suitable replacements can be tough.

As such, Arsenal are likely to look at the market to see what they can do. However, it looks like there could still be a forward brought in.

This is an important distinction. The point being made here is that whilst there is speculation around one particular player, Romano has indicated that Arsenal can strengthen their attacking options. This is not to say that any particular move is happening.

If Arteta keeps Hincapié, then Arsenal will maintain their defensive stability and possibly gain another option in attack.

Over the next few weeks we will get a better understanding of what is available to them. But for now it looks like Arsenal are not keen to lose Hincapié but will look to strengthen their attacking options if the right deal comes along.

#2098. Liverpool told they have a ‘high energy’ dynamo who gives them ‘a chance’ of being in a title race

One player could give Liverpool ‘a chance’ of being involved in the Premier League title race this season if he stays ‘fit and available’.

That’s according to Troy Deeney, who made such claims about Alexis Mac Allister after another standout performance from the midfielder in the Reds’ 3-1 victory over Crystal Palace on Sunday.

The Argentine opened the scoring with a close-range header shortly before half-time, taking his goal tally for the season to five and matching his haul from the entirety of 2025/26.

Despite the 27-year-old’s consistently top-class displays, he’s yet to be offered a new contract by Liverpool, a situation which has baffled Deeney.

The former Watford striker selected Mac Allister in his Premier League team of the week for BBC Sport, outlining: ‘How this guy has not got a new deal I don’t know. He keeps coming up big, high energy, and he is vitally important to the way Liverpool play.

‘When he is fit and available and firing on all cylinders, it feels like they have a chance in this title race.’

(Photo by Justin Setterfield/Getty Images)

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

The World Cup winner was one of the Reds’ biggest disappointments in a chastening 2025/26 season for the club, but he’s been among the standout performers for his team since Andoni Iraola took the reins from Arne Slot over the summer.

Not only is Mac Allister chipping in with a steady supply of goals, he also showed his commitment to the cause and to his teammates by confronting Jefferson Lerma in the closing stages when the Palace player got involved in a spat with young Trey Nyoni.

With a gap of nine points to make up on leaders Arsenal, it’ll take a remarkable level of consistency from Liverpool to haul themselves into genuine title contention going into the latter weeks of the campaign.

Could our number 10 potentially elevate us into the title picture if he maintains this standard of performance? In truth it’ll require the rest of his teammates to follow suit, rather than being overly depending on just one or two individuals.

For now, though, Mac Allister just needs to keep up what he’s been doing since August and mind the disciplinary tightrope – he’s one yellow card away from a suspension in the Premier League, and two in the Champions League.

On current form, Liverpool can’t afford to be without the Argentine for long.

#2097. Danny Drinkwater on Chelsea move he ‘could never turn down’

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Danny Drinkwater Chelsea story still carries a sting. Five years at Stamford Bridge gave him plenty of time to think about how it all turned out, and his own verdict is not a happy one.

Drinkwater opened up in a Truth or Padel appearance alongside David Ornstein. He said the move was one he could never have turned down, even with the benefit of hindsight. “No, I don’t regret moving. I regret, in hindsight, how it’s turned out. I think I could never turn it down,” he said.

Strip away the headlines and the numbers alone tell an uncomfortable story. Drinkwater spent five years on Chelsea’s books. In that time he made just 23 appearances, according to the original interview report.

Do the maths and it barely comes to five games a year. That is a thin return for a central midfielder signed to compete for a first-team place. It is also the kind of record that rarely sits well at a club of Chelsea’s size and ambition.

Chelsea’s best attacking spells over a similar stretch of seasons look far more productive, as our striker rankings show. Set next to those numbers, Drinkwater’s five quiet years stand out even more starkly.

Five years. Twenty three games. These numbers alone explains why this transfer still gets talked about by Drinkwater, long after the headlines faded.

The numbers only tell part of the story. A string of incidents turned a quiet, underwhelming spell into something far more pointed.

Drinkwater had a training-ground bust-up while out on loan. He was also involved in an on-pitch brawl with Chelsea’s own under-23s side. Flashpoints between a first-team player and a development squad are rare at any club, let alone one with Chelsea’s standards.

Taken alone, either incident might have passed as a bad day. Taken together, it became harder to see Drinkwater simply as an unlucky squad player who could not catch a break.

The most serious moment came during his time at Aston Villa. Drinkwater headbutted team-mate Jota and was fined two weeks’ wages for it. He says the consequences at Chelsea did not stop there.

“They didn’t formally ban me, but I got removed from the first team building after the incident at Villa,” Drinkwater said.

One off-field incident, at another club, pushed him out of the manager’s day-to-day plans back home. No formal punishment at Chelsea followed. The effect was much the same.

Chelsea fans saw a similar breakdown of trust play out when Graham Potter struggled to win over the dressing room during another difficult Chelsea transition. Trust can break down fast once results or behaviour turn against the person at the centre of it.

Drinkwater’s comments matter beyond his own career. They are a reminder that saying yes to a big move does not guarantee it will work once a player walks through the door.

That tension has not gone away. Chelsea’s current transfer business, covered in our transfer watch, shows the club still weighing big calls on players whose futures depend on more than form alone.

Squad management under Xabi Alonso, assessed in our bomb squad verdict, raises a familiar issue. Players who fall outside a manager’s plans rarely find a quick way back in, whether the exile is formal or not.

Drinkwater never found his way back into Chelsea’s plans after the Villa incident. His account of the Danny Drinkwater Chelsea years stands as a blunt warning. A move that looks impossible to refuse can still become one of the hardest spells of a career.

#2095. Arsenal vs Hull City preview: Arteta sticks with winning formula as Gunners target another three points

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have resisted the temptation to rotate after their victory at Anfield, with Mikel Arteta naming the same starting XI for Saturday’s meeting with Hull City.

Arsenal welcome Championship side Hull City to the Emirates Stadium, looking to strengthen their grip on the top spot after collecting 24 points from their opening nine Premier League games. However, their tally of just 14 goals suggests Arteta’s side still have work to do in the final third.

Arteta has again chosen to partner Ezri Konsa and Gabriel Magalhães in defence. William Saliba was left out despite pre-match talk that he would be named in the starting XI. Bukayo Saka and Max Dowman are also missing from the squad.

Noni Madueke will carry the right. Tzolis starts on the opposite flank. Kai Havertz will lead the line. Viktor Gyökeres, Eberechi Eze and Mikel Merino add attacking options from the bench.

Arsenal predicted starting XI: Raya; Timber, Konsa, Gabriel, Calafiori; Guimarães, Rice, Ødegaard; Madueke, Havertz, Tzolis.

Substitutes: Arrizabalaga, White, Mosquera, Hincapié, Lewis-Skelly, Zubimendi, Merino, Eze, Gyökeres.

Hull are coming into this game ranked 11th in the league after five draws in their first nine games. They’ve not won in their last eight games but can cause problems for any opposition.

They look like playing a defensive game with Slater, Iroegbunam and Gourna-Douath in the midfield to protect the backline. McBurnie and Cho will give them some attacking options.

Hull starting XI: Tzolakis; Coyle, Ajayi, Egan, Mendy, Stroud; Slater, Iroegbunam, Gourna-Douath; McBurnie, Cho.

Substitutes: Butland, Giles, Thomas, Gelhardt, Hjerto-Dahl, Norton-Cuffy, Herrington, Mouzakitis, Vaz.

McBurnie is just one booking away from suspension for the clash against Brighton on November 22 but may make an important impact in the physical battle with Gabriel.

Hull have not scored at the Emirates since 2014 but will look to turn that around. However, their main concern will be to ensure they keep Arsenal quiet for as long as possible.

Arsenal will need to find ways to break down the likely tight Hull backline.

Ødegaard and Rice will be key in keeping possession and creating chances, whilst Madueke and Tzolis will create width and movement.

Hull may be exposed to threat in case they are trapped in defence for too long, but Arteta can use Gyökeres if Havertz is having difficulties doing anything productive.

A win for Arsenal will give them a six-point lead. Manchester City will be looking to get all three points when they face Nottingham Forest later on Saturday.

The visitors will look to create an uncomfortable afternoon for the Gunners.

Arsenal have enough quality to take all three points, but Hull will not make it easy for them. I expect this to be a tight game, and the Gunners will win by a small margin.

#2094. Chelsea sweat on key stars as Bournemouth test looms

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

The main concern revolves around Joao Pedro. Chelsea’s current player of the season rescued a point against newly promoted Hull, but he suffered a knock and sat out the damaging 3-0 defeat at Brentford. The problem also prompted his removal from Brazil’s squad shortly after he had returned to it.

Chelsea badly need a lift. Defeat at Brentford before the break left Alonso’s side looking shaky, and a squad stretched by injuries has not helped.

Saturday brings a chance to steady things. If Palmer, James and Caicedo are available, with Palestra and Emegha potentially adding fresh options, the picture will look much brighter at Stamford Bridge. Joao Pedro’s absence will still sting. However, a strong response against Bournemouth would go a long way towards easing the pressure and restoring belief.

#2093. Arsenal 1-0 Hull City: Martin Ødegaard rescues Gunners with late winner at the Emirates

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal needed a moment of quality from their captain to overcome Hull City and claim a narrow 1-0 Premier League victory at the Emirates Stadium.

Martin Ødegaard scored in the 72nd minute as the hosts struggled to break down a resolute Hull side. It was much like I expected in my match preview, as Arsenal had to work hard to win this game.

The result gives them an extended lead at the top of the table. Ahead of Manchester City’s clash with Nottingham Forest. However, Mikel Arteta will be aware that his team did not play well in this game. It was far from a walk in the park.

Hull arrived at London looking to play an attacking game. Jakirovic’s side were well organised and looked to launch quick counter attacks.

They did have some positive moments in the first half, but Arsenal struggled to get into their attacking rhythm. Kai Havertz couldn’t capitalise on one good chance created by Ødegaard.

The hosts were hopeful of taking the lead after Havertz fell to the ground in the box after being pulled back by Nobel Mendy.

However, referee Farai Hallam’s initial decision was overturned after a VAR review showed Havertz was offside earlier in the attack, so Arsenal’s penalty was cancelled.

Another frustrating moment for the home side, who failed to score during the first half, despite dominating possession.

Arsenal came out strong after the break. However, Hull were still causing problems. Lewie Coyle was also walking a fine line after a yellow card in the first half and a late challenge on Christos Tzolis.

Arteta made a three-man change on the hour mark, bringing on Viktor Gyökeres and Mikel Merino for Havertz and Declan Rice. Six minutes later he brought on Eberechi Eze and Piero Hincapié.

The breakthrough came from Madueke, forcing Konstantinos Tzolakis into action. The Hull keeper couldn’t hold his shot, and Ødegaard was quickest to react, scoring from the rebound.

The captain’s strike took the frustration out of Arsenal’s efforts and was a reward for their perseverance. It also provided another vital moment for the Norwegian. He’s had a tough campaign so far due to injuries.

Despite going behind, Hull never gave up in their search for an equaliser. However, Arsenal held on to their lead.

Arsenal dominated the game with 67% of possession and 21 shots, compared to just seven for Hull. Tzolakis put in a number of key interventions to keep the scoreline respectable.

Although they weren’t at their best, Arsenal were able to perform in the key moments of the game. Ødegaard’s goal meant that the Gunners earned three points and are sitting top of the Premier League heading into the international break.

#2092. Chelsea co-sporting director Laurence Stewart to leave in leadership reshuffle

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Laurence Stewart is set to leave Chelsea by mutual consent after almost four years at Stamford Bridge, with the club opting to redistribute his responsibilities rather than appoint a direct replacement.

The departure is understood to be amicable and won’t take immediate effect. Stewart could continue attending matches before formally leaving, following discussions with co-owner Behdad Eghbali over the club’s evolving sporting structure.

The story was first reported by Matt Law of The Telegraph and Nizaar Kinsella of BBC Sport, with Ben Jacobs subsequently providing further details about how Chelsea intend to operate.

Stewart arrived from Monaco in October 2022 as technical director before becoming co-sporting director alongside Paul Winstanley in February 2023.

Chelsea’s day-to-day sporting leadership will now be shared between Winstanley, Sam Jewell and Joe Shields, each with defined responsibilities.

Winstanley will continue overseeing transfer negotiations, player contracts and several departments at Cobham, including medical and performance operations. Shields’ work centres on the academy and player pathways, while Jewell has wider responsibilities across the BlueCo operation, including Strasbourg.

According to Jacobs, discussions about restructuring began in April and accelerated following the appointment of Xabi Alonso as head coach.

Stewart had overseen investment in Chelsea’s data analysis operation, although Jewell and Shields have increasingly taken responsibility for using that information in recruitment and talent identification.

Dave Fallows will remain involved in an advisory capacity, rather than taking on a fixed day-to-day portfolio.

The changes are being described as a natural evolution of Chelsea’s sporting model. There is no suggestion that Stewart’s departure followed a breakdown in relations, and the club are not currently planning to recruit a replacement.

#2090. Arsenal still tracking long-term striker target, door still open

Graeme Hanna is the site lead of Read Rangers and also writes about West Ham for Read West Ham. A Rangers season-ticket holder and freelance writer, he has previously written for Vital Rangers, Rangers Review, Football Ground Guide and Give Me Sport, and has a long association with the Follow Follow fanzine.

Arsenal are still monitoring Bayer Leverkusen striker Christian Kofane as they weigh up options to strengthen their attack, according to BBC Sport.

The Gunners already know the 20-year-old well.

Kofane featured for Leverkusen when the sides met in last season’s Champions League round of 16, when Mikel Arteta’s team progressed 3-1 on aggregate.

The renewed interest follows heavy speculation during the summer.

In April, Kofane’s agent Eric Depolo said Arsenal’s interest was genuine and confirmed he was in contact with the club, claiming Kofane would give them a top striker for the next decade.

Leverkusen were reported to value him at around €70m, though Depolo had talked up a €100m figure. The Gunners were said to be ‘in pole position’, but no move materialised.

Arsenal wanted a forward in the last window but missed out on Vinicius Jr and Julian Alvarez, while Morgan Rogers joined Chelsea instead.

Kofane’s rise has been rapid. He moved from AS Nylon in Cameroon to Albacete in 2024, scoring eight goals in 20 Segunda Division games, before joining Leverkusen in July 2025.

He made 44 appearances in his first season in Germany, including 11 in the Champions League, and has three goals in six games this term. The frontman is currently sidelined with a hamstring injury.

If and when, the striker leaves North Rhine-Westphalia, Arsenal face competition.

Manchester United, Real Madrid and Bayern Munich are all reported to be tracking him, amid reports his contract talks with Leverkusen have stalled.

The striker situation at the Emirates adds context. Viktor Gyokeres scored 21 goals last season but has been second choice behind Kai Havertz and has started only once in the league.

A January deal looks unlikely unless Arsenal sell one of their current forwards first.

For now, Kofane remains a long-term target rather than an imminent signing, but Arsenal’s interest has clearly not faded.

#2088. Hungarian federation issues Dominik Szoboszlai update as Liverpool left sweating on his fitness

The Hungarian FA has issued an update on the fitness of Dominik Szoboszlai ahead of their upcoming UEFA Nations League fixtures.

There is an injury concern over the midfielder after he sprained his knee in Liverpool’s 3-1 victory over Crystal Palace on Sunday, with Andoni Iraola unsure as to whether or not the 26-year-old would be able to represent his country during this international window.

The Magyars are away to Georgia this coming Saturday and then host Northern Ireland three days later in what could be a straight shootout for promotion to League A on the final matchday.

On Tuesday, the Hungarian FA published an update via X in which they confirmed that a decision on Szoboszlai won’t be taken until the latest possible opportunity.

They stated: ‘Dominik Szoboszlai has undergone the required medical examinations. Following his knee sprain, the national team’s medical staff will do everything possible over the coming days to ensure that the team captain is available to the coaching staff as soon as possible.

‘However, a decision on his availability for the match will almost certainly only be made at the last minute.’

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

As captain of his country, the 26-year-old will no doubt be striving to prove his fitness in time for the match against Georgia on Saturday, or at least be in a position to play against Northern Ireland.

However, those of a Liverpool perspective will surely be hoping that Szoboszlai isn’t risked in either game if he isn’t 100% fit to feature, as he’ll be very much needed at Anfield going into the unforgiving winter schedule.

The Reds still have 11 more matches to play in 2026, starting with a grudge fixture at home to Manchester United on Sunday week, and squad depth and rotation will be essential during that hectic period in the run-up to Christmas and New Year’s.

Having only recently returned from a thigh issue, Andoni Iraola will be hoping that this doesn’t turn into a season of frequent interruptions for the usually dependable Hungarian, who before this autumn hadn’t missed a game through injury since February 2024 (Transfermarkt).

Whatever the decision on Szoboszlai might be in the coming days, fingers crossed that he’ll be fully fit and firing in time for the Red Devils’ visit to Anfield in 12 days’ time.

#2087. Chelsea v Bournemouth: Kick-off time, TV details and how to follow

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

There will be no live UK television coverage of Chelsea’s return to Premier League action against Bournemouth on Saturday afternoon.

Xabi Alonso’s side host the Cherries at Stamford Bridge in a traditional 3pm kick-off, with the fixture falling within the Saturday afternoon broadcasting blackout.

That means supporters unable to attend will need to follow the match through other channels, rather than watching it live on Sky Sports or TNT Sports.

Although the game will not be televised live in the UK, supporters can follow developments through the official Chelsea website and the Premier League website.

The Saturday 3pm blackout prevents live domestic television broadcasts during the restricted period, although overseas coverage may be available through broadcasters holding the relevant rights.

Highlights will be available after the match through the usual broadcast and digital channels, subject to their respective release schedules.

Chelsea will then turn their attention to a trip to Everton on Saturday, 17 October, with that fixture scheduled for a 12.30pm kick-off at Hill Dickinson Stadium.

For Alonso, the Bournemouth match provides the immediate opportunity to resume Chelsea’s domestic campaign following the international break.

#2085. William Saliba earns Arsenal U21s praise as defender continues comeback from injury

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

William Saliba Arsenal return: William Saliba has made a great impression during his return to action, according to Arsenal’s coaching staff. The U21s manager, David Horseman, praising his attitude and professionalism.

The 25-year-old played the first 45 minutes of Arsenal U21’s 5-2 win at Boreham Wood against Aston Villa on Sunday. Slowly making his way back from his three-and-a-half-month layoff.

Saliba was clearly a quality player, but it was his attitude which impressed Horseman. He worked well with the younger players and was a great example to follow throughout the morning.

The defender had been scheduled to play, and there were no new injury concerns behind his half-time withdrawal. He will return to the first team group as he continues to build up match fitness.

Saliba was praised by Horseman for his positive attitude since joining the club. His preparation, involvement in team meetings and ability to communicate with the younger players stood out.

He also showed his quality with the ball. His passing range helped the Arsenal attack and he made a good weighted pass that led to Marcell Washington running into space before Andre Harriman-Annous’ first goal.

Saliba didn’t see it as below his level to play in the game and really got into helping the academy players.

It set an example for the younger players in the squad. With an established first teamer keeping up the standards, there wasn’t much of an excuse for anyone else not to.

Saliba was not the only senior player to feature, with Marli Salmon as captain and Max Dowman also playing. Dowman, Maalik Hashi and Evan Mooney all scored, and Harriman-Annous added two goals.

It was another key step in Saliba’s return, as he featured in the matchday squad for Arsenal’s game against Slavia Prague after playing 45 minutes in the behind-closed-doors friendly.

He wasn’t selected in the senior squad for their game against Hull City. With only nine subs allowed in the Premier League, Arsenal decided it was best to play him for the U21s.

The decision means that Saliba has been able to play competitive minutes to build up his fitness.

Arsenal will be hoping that he can now make a full recovery from his injury so that Mikel Arteta has another defensive option heading into a busy period for the club.

However, the U21s will have to maintain their high standards from the Aston Villa game without first team players such as Saliba, Salmon and Dowman.

We’ll keep you updated as and when we hear more information about the William Saliba Arsenal return.

#2084. Time to make the best use of West Ham’s talisman – before we wear him out

Strikers, you would think, tend to have longer careers than wingers. West Ham’s own top scorer in the Premier League was himself a converted full back-stroke-winger who became the ‘beast’ that was Michael Antonio. And the chances are, he’d have seen his career curtailed long before the car crash that almost cost him his life, if he’d been hurtling up and down the wing all afternoon, twice a week for half a dozen years.

As it was, his hamstrings needed their own private treatment regime but West Ham managed to keep him in one piece long enough for him to notch sixty eight Premier League goals – still just a club record.

But it got me thinking, Jarrod Bowen hits thirty this year. You can see where I’m going with this.

West Ham are too reliant upon Jarrod Bowen and need to plan to help him work smarter, not just harder.

The player who David Moyes signed in 2020 has won us a trophy, run his legs off and contributed blood, sweat, toil and tears for this club and still he’s there every match day, tackling back, doing his defensive duties, then haring up the other end and assisting or bearing down on goal himself. Oh and we made him skipper too just for good measure. An awful lot on our star man’s shoulders.

Is that the best use of Bowen as he leaves his ‘twenties behind? West Ham have no understudy, no support act ready to step into that right wing position to give him a breather or a chance to move centrally and become the ‘converted’ striker his first West Ham manager David Moyes used to churn out – be it Antonio, Arnautovic or, back at Everton now, Thierno Barry, his latest ‘convert’  to the striker role.

Perhaps Bowen needs a more central role? Perhaps West Ham need another winger to offer Scott Parker the chance to move Bowen inside, as he does with Manor Solomon on the left.

After all, our leading scorer and ‘assists’ king can’t influence games if he’s mired in tracking back down at the wrong end of the pitch. And he’s not getting any younger.

#2083. Chelsea’s interest in Alex Scott remains despite Bournemouth transfer stance

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Bournemouth’s determination to keep Alex Scott has done little to discourage Chelsea, who remain among the leading contenders to sign the England midfielder.

The Blues had a £64m bid rejected during the summer, but their interest has continued into the new season. According to Matt Law of The Telegraph, Chelsea are at the front of the queue of clubs monitoring the 23-year-old.

Bournemouth, however, have no intention of allowing Scott to leave in January. talkSPORT reported this week that the south coast club remain determined to retain him, despite his reluctance to sign a contract extension.

Scott’s current deal runs until 2028, leaving Bournemouth with time to negotiate, although their position could become more complicated if no agreement is reached before next summer.

The midfielder’s immediate priority is recovering from a thigh injury sustained while representing England. He impressed during the 7-0 Nations League victory over Croatia but was withdrawn after 70 minutes. Subsequent examinations revealed a quadriceps problem, with BBC Sport reporting that he could miss six to eight weeks.

His absence is a setback for Bournemouth, who have relied heavily on the former Bristol City midfielder this season. It also introduces another consideration for clubs monitoring his progress ahead of the January window.

Chelsea are not alone in their admiration. Arsenal and Liverpool have also been linked with Scott, whose performances have attracted attention beyond the Premier League.

Suggestions circulating on social media that Bournemouth have already agreed to let him leave in January remain unsubstantiated. There has been no reliable confirmation of such an arrangement, and the club’s reported position is firmly against a winter sale.

For Chelsea, the more realistic opportunity may come next summer, particularly if Scott continues to resist signing a new contract.

#2082. Chelsea make early move for Palmeiras teenager Eduardo Conceicao

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Chelsea have reportedly made contact with Palmeiras as they attempt to gain an early advantage in the race to sign Brazilian teenager Eduardo Conceicao.

The 16-year-old forward has attracted attention from several leading European clubs, but Chelsea are understood to be among the first to establish direct contact with Palmeiras over a potential future transfer.

According to Mark Brus of CaughtOffside, the London club have held a series of discussions as they seek to position themselves ahead of their rivals.

Palmeiras are reportedly preparing for substantial offers, with some figures at the Brazilian club discussing a valuation as high as €100m (£85m).

That figure has not been established as a firm asking price, however, and Brus reports that industry sources expect the eventual cost to be lower. Chelsea’s previous investment of approximately £51m in Estevao is regarded as a possible benchmark.

Manchester City’s ownership group have also made contact regarding Conceicao, while Arsenal, Manchester United, Barcelona, Real Madrid, Bayern Munich and Paris Saint-Germain are among the clubs reported to have watched him.

Chelsea’s existing relationship with Palmeiras could help their efforts, although the level of competition means there is no guarantee that their early interest will lead to a deal.

The player’s age is another consideration. Conceicao cannot complete an international transfer to England until he turns 18, leaving interested clubs facing a potentially lengthy wait before he can join their squads.

Chelsea have already demonstrated their willingness to agree deals for young players well before they are eligible to arrive, with Estevao providing a particularly relevant example.

No formal bid or agreement has been reported for Conceicao. Nevertheless, the latest claims suggest Chelsea are prepared to explore another long-term investment in Brazilian talent rather than wait until the player becomes available for an immediate move.

#2081. Atletico Madrid eye January move for Arsenal midfielder Martin Zubimendi

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Atletico Madrid are looking to make an ambitious January swoop for Martin Zubimendi after the Arsenal midfielder failed to convince Mikel Arteta to give him a regular starting spot.

The Spain international has started all seven of his Premier League appearances this season, coming off the bench. This has reportedly caught the eye of Diego Simeone, who wants to bolster his midfield in the winter window.

Atletico have marked Zubimendi as a possible target, according to Spanish radio station RNE via SPORT. Sporting director Mateu Alemany feels that there is still more to do with Simeone’s squad despite big investments in new players this summer.

If Zubimendi’s situation fails to improve at Arsenal, he could become one of the most wanted signings of January.

Zubimendi played a major role in Arsenal’s Premier League title-winning campaign last season. He made 34 league starts, scoring five goals and providing one assist.

He played a key role in the club’s run to the Champions League final. Since then Arsenal’s midfield has changed.

Injuries have hampered his campaign, but Myles Lewis-Skelly has returned to the midfield, and Bruno Guimaraes joined in the summer.

The 27-year-old has also expressed frustration at the lack of playing opportunities available to him. He was signed for £60 million from Real Sociedad to Arsenal just 18 months ago and has three-and-a-half years left on his contract.

Any move to Atletico would be a battle. Real Madrid are also reportedly fans of the midfielder, and he has also been linked with a move to Chelsea in recent months.

Atletico are keen to bring Zubimendi in alongside Pablo Barrios in central midfield.

It would be a dream combination for Simeone, and the Spanish club are willing to fund any move.

Another option could see him involved in a deal for Julian Alvarez. There are currently no talks about a potential swap deal.

Arteta has previously played down concerns about Zubimendi’s importance to Arsenal. Earlier this month, he highlighted the midfielder’s commitment and suggested injuries had affected his opportunities.

The Arsenal manager also made clear that Zubimendi would have chances to play, but would need to take them when they arrived.

That leaves the midfielder with an opportunity to work his way back into Arteta’s plans. However, Atletico’s reported interest could add another dimension to his situation as January approaches.

It has taken three years for West Ham to spiral from the summit of their footballing prowess to the depths of second division mediocrity, and the Hammers’ experiences can serve as a cautionary tale to any other poorly run Premier League side. Complacency and poor decision making will destroy even the best – supported side in the blink of  an eye.

Hammers fans have also taken years to process the sale of their most successful captain since Billy Bonds, the Chairman announcing his availability the very day after their Europa Conference League trophy sale. For that alone, I’ll never forgive Sullivan.

There are ways of doing things. and that isn’t it.

As supporters we are now getting used to seeing Declan Rice in an Arsenal kit, and for many – most, I’d guess, but not all.. there’s a vicarious sense of pride still for the former Hammer, although of course not his club, as he goes onto deserved success.

It is clear the players’ love for West Ham hasn’t diminished either. Speaking to GQ magazine, Declan professed:

““I obviously love West Ham so much still, still a massive part of my life, I still watch them.”

Picking out his favourite goal ever, Rice glossed over those Champions League free kicks and confessed a goal in Claret and Blue meant more to him:

“My favourite goal is when I was playing for West Ham in the Conference League,” Rice said. “I picked the ball up on the edge of our own box and ran the whole pitch. As I was running forward I just remember the stadium getting louder and louder and louder, and as I scored the goal the place just erupted.

So I think that was one of my best solo goals.”

How fast and how much things have changed in the ensuing three years.  The only comfort for West Ham fans is that they can change back again just as fast. COYI!

#2079. Chelsea news: Stewart departure, Scott interest and Bournemouth preparations

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. Chelsea’s sporting department is facing another change following news of Laurence Stewart’s impending departure, while the club continue to be linked with potential additions to their squad.

Alex Scott and Palmeiras teenager Eduardo Conceicao have both featured in Thursday’s transfer coverage, with Xabi Alonso also preparing his players for Saturday’s Premier League meeting with Bournemouth.

Here is our 7pm round-up of the latest Chelsea news for Thursday, 8 October.

Laurence Stewart is set to leave Chelsea by mutual consent after almost four years at Stamford Bridge, with the club planning to redistribute his responsibilities rather than appoint a direct replacement.

His departure is understood to be amicable and will not take immediate effect. Stewart joined from Monaco in 2022, initially as technical director, before becoming co-sporting director alongside Paul Winstanley.

As detailed in our report on Stewart’s departure and Chelsea’s leadership reshuffle, Winstanley, Sam Jewell and Joe Shields are expected to take on expanded responsibilities across recruitment, player development and the wider BlueCo operation.

The changes follow discussions about the club’s sporting structure that reportedly began earlier this year. There is no confirmed suggestion that Stewart’s departure resulted from a disagreement with Alonso.

Chelsea’s reported interest in Bournemouth midfielder Alex Scott remains a talking point, despite the south coast club’s determination to retain the England international.

The Blues reportedly had a £64m offer rejected during the summer, with Matt Law of The Telegraph subsequently identifying Chelsea as leading contenders for the 23-year-old.

Our report on Chelsea’s interest in Eduardo Conceicao explains that a valuation as high as €100m has been suggested, although that figure is not a confirmed asking price or evidence of a formal bid.

Manchester City, Arsenal and Real Madrid are among the other clubs reportedly monitoring the teenager. Conceicao would be unable to complete a transfer to England until he turns 18, making any prospective move a longer-term consideration.

Alonso’s attention will now turn to Saturday’s Premier League meeting with Bournemouth at Stamford Bridge.

The match kicks off at 3pm and will not be televised live in the United Kingdom because of the Saturday afternoon broadcasting blackout.

Our Chelsea v Bournemouth kick-off and how-to-watch guide provides the full details, including how to follow the match and the available overseas broadcast information.

Scott’s injury is expected to deprive Bournemouth of an important midfield option, although Chelsea will have their own fitness decisions to make as players return from international duty.

After Saturday’s fixture, Chelsea travel to Hill Dickinson Stadium to face Everton on 17 October. Alonso will be looking for greater consistency as the domestic programme resumes.

#2077. Arsenal’s Havertz penalty controversy sparks fresh call for change to football laws

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal’s controversial penalty claim against Hull City has raised new questions about how the offside rule is interpreted in football.

Former Premier League official Dermot Gallagher believes that whilst he understands why the officials made the decision, the result would have been different under the current rules.

Speaking on Ref Watch on Sky Sports, Gallagher said that the holding should have been the main focus of the incident involving Kai Havertz and Hull defender Nobel Mendy.

His comments have increased criticism of referee Farai Hallam and VAR official Michael Oliver. However, Gallagher also feels that the language used in the laws needs to be addressed to avoid this issue in the future.

The incident happened shortly before half-time during Arsenal’s 1-0 victory over Hull at the Emirates Stadium.

Mendy pulled Havertz’s shirt inside the penalty area, prompting VAR to recommend an on-field review. Hallam subsequently confirmed that the Hull defender had committed a holding offence.

However, he ruled that Havertz was in an offside position and awarded Hull a free-kick.

Gallagher believes that was incorrect under the laws of the game. He explained that being in an offside position does not automatically constitute an offence.

The player must be involved in play before the referee can give a penalty. In this instance, Havertz had not tried to play the ball or challenge an opponent before being pulled back by Mendy.

This means that the player was held first, so a penalty should have been given to Arsenal.

Gallagher said that the AR had first raised their flag for offside. He said that it was a great process with VAR, but the initial decision could have affected the outcome.

He recognised why the decision was made even though he felt Arsenal should have been awarded the penalty.

In terms of common sense, he said it does not look right to award the penalty, as Havertz was in an offside position and couldn’t legally contest for the ball.

But he emphasised that the officials must strictly enforce the laws of the game. Gallagher thinks there is some grey space here which can be addressed by having more specific wording.

This could be crucial in these types of situations as the difference between being in an offside position and committing an offside offence can have a big effect on whether a penalty is awarded.

It’s a small comfort for Arsenal after missing the chance to take the lead from the spot. But Martin Odegaard did eventually score the winner in the 72nd minute to give Mikel Arteta’s men three points.

The incident has exposed a wider problem for referees. Until the laws provide clearer guidance, similar decisions could continue to generate controversy.

#2076. Coutinho 2.0? Liverpool make their move for Brazilian starlet after recent contract decision

Almost 14 years on from signing Philippe Coutinho, Liverpool are reportedly making moves to potentially sign another Brazilian playmaker who’s been attracting some high-profile European interest.

The Reds’ former number 10 spent five years at Anfield, during which time he scored 54 goals in 201 appearances and was a hugely influential figure on the pitch, frequently conjuring up moments of majesty.

He was sold in January 2018 and wasn’t around for the peak years of the Jurgen Klopp reign, although there’s an up-and-coming talent from Brazil who LFC will be hoping can become synonymous with a successful era under Andoni Iraola.

According to Sport Witness, Liverpool have made official enquiries with Palmeiras about 19-year-old attacking midfielder Erick Bele, with the Brazilian club prepared to start negotiations at €30m (£25m).

Paris Saint-Germain have also been in contact regarding the teenager, who signed a contract extension to 2031 with his current employers last week.

The youngster stood out during a recent training camp for Brazil under-20s in which he provided three assists across wins over Denmark, United States and Mexico.

Bele has an impressive record at youth level for Palmeiras, with 20 goals in 45 games for their under-20 side, although his development is being managed carefully as he’s made just seven senior appearances so far (Transfermarkt).

He plays mostly as a number 10 but can also be deployed on the right flank, similar to Harvey Elliott, and it attests to how highly his club value him that they’ve rewarded him with such a lengthy new contract.

An analysis from Future Ballers cites finishing, pace, dribbling, technique, heading, off-the-ball movement and creativity among his strongest attributes, marking him out as a well-rounded young talent who also boasts plenty of physical strength.

Even if Liverpool were to sign Bele in 2027, realistically he wouldn’t be challenging for a first-team place straight away, given his inexperience at senior level and the presence of Florian Wirtz in the Reds’ XI.

A loan move or two to clubs in mainland Europe would likely be arranged in order to enhance his development, but we can certainly see why Anfield chiefs have enquired about an exciting prospect who, in time, could have strong echoes of Coutinho.
