#2073. David Raya names Arsenal teammate as dressing room’s worst DJ in revealing teammate verdict

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

David Raya has offered a glimpse into the lighter side of Arsenal’s dressing room. He’s revealed which teammates make the most noise, take training too seriously and have the worst taste in music.

The goalkeeper was in a jovial mood during his Q&A with GOAL. And offered his thoughts on a number of players in Mikel Arteta’s squad.

When asked who he thought was the worst DJ in the dressing room, Raya was quick to point out Noni Madueke.

But the Spaniard also revealed that the team have a far from relaxing time travelling abroad. With a number of Arsenal stars always making an impact on the bus.

One of the more entertaining answers came from Raya, as he named Madueke as the player with the worst music taste.

But he wasn’t the only one to get a bit of friendly ribbing.

Raya said the three that make the most noise in their away games are Eberechi Eze, Madueke and Bukayo Saka.

They love playing Uno on the bus. However, they have such a competitive nature that it is rarely quiet.

It also gives a good insight into the character within the Arsenal squad. As the goalkeeper has said, there is a lot of character in the group.

Raya also mentioned the high standards in the Arsenal training environment.

He said that Mikel Merino is the player who hates losing the most due to his natural competitive nature.

Merino was also Raya’s choice to cook him a meal.

Raya was not particularly impressed with Max Dowman’s culinary abilities and indicated that the young player has much to improve in the kitchen.

He also disclosed some of the antics of the players off the field. Tommy Setford was picked as the player who occupies the front of the mirror for the longest time, while Piero Hincapie received the title of the most active player on TikTok.

One of the more unusual questions Raya was asked was if he would pick a teammate to be stuck in a desert with.

He said he would pick Jurrien Timber due to his intelligence. This is a glowing compliment for the Dutch defender who has become an important part of Arteta’s defensive options.

The answers given by Raya give us a fun insight into the relationships within the Arsenal squad. Whilst the players are fighting for major honours, there is clearly no shortage of banter between them.

#2072. Arsenal Could Benefit as PSG Step Away from Ibrahim Maza Transfer Race

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal could have been given a helping hand in their quest to sign Ibrahim Maza. Paris Saint-Germain reportedly pulls out of talks with Bayer Leverkusen’s attacker.

The Gunners are just one of several clubs keeping an eye on the exciting young talent in Germany. However, with some of the biggest clubs in Europe keen on the prospect of adding the player to their attack, it might have made it difficult for them.

PSG looked like genuine contenders for the signing of Maza. The French champions having reportedly had talks with both Maza and Leverkusen.

It seems that PSG are not interested anymore, according to Fichajes. They have ended negotiations with the player and Leverkusen because they were unhappy with how the player’s representatives and Leverkusen have conducted themselves during the talks.

This could be great news for Arsenal, but it doesn’t mean they have full control of the situation.

The withdrawal of a club of PSG’s calibre is a benefit for Arsenal. They had seemed well on their way to landing Maza. Now, they will pursue him without the pressure of competition.

Maza has emerged as a good signing for clubs looking to improve the flair in their attacking third. Leverkusen will have noticed this and may be tempted to sell him if they get a decent offer.

Arsenal might also determine if Maza would be a valuable asset for their team in the long run. They must ensure that he can adapt to their attacking strategies.

They will not be the only one interested in Maza. Although PSG may be willing to let him go, there are many other clubs interested in the player.

Leverkusen will also want to get the best deal out of the situation. If they are willing to negotiate, Arsenal need to think if it would make financial sense to do so.

Finally, it will come down to what the player wants. Arsenal would need to persuade the player that the Emirates is the right place for his progression and that they can provide the right route for his aspirations and ambitions.

This may be a step in the right direction for Arsenal.

If Maza is still a target for the Gunners, then they may have a better chance of getting it done given PSG’s new stance. But they will still need to come to an agreement with Leverkusen and convince the player to move to north London.

#2071. Now made public Manchester United set to sack Michael Carrick

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

It has now been made public that Michael Carrick is to be sacked by Manchester United.

The Athletic having announced this as breaking news.

Manchester United head into late November sitting in the relegation zone, they have only won one Premier League match all season  and none since August.

In all competitions Man U have failed to win 12 of their 15 matches and their only victories have been against minnows Sabah and very lucky wins against Ipswich and Roma after having trailed in both of those games.

The Athletic say that publicly Manchester United are still insisting no decision has been taken on Michael Carrick.

However, they say that the reality is very different behind the scenes.

The Athletic reporting that ‘on the condition of anonymity to protect relationships’, multiple reliable sources have told them that senior staff are working frantically behind the scenes to have a replacement for Michael Carrick appointed before their next match.

Using the international break as the chance to do so, ahead of a trip to…Anfield. The last thing Manchester United need right now, a likely humiliation against hated rivals Liverpool, on top of everything else.

The report says that once senior staff have their chosen candidate in place, it will then be up to the Glazers and Sir Jim Ratcliffe to rubber stamp the sacking of Michael Carrick and the introduction of his replacement.

The Athletic report their understanding of three leading candidates the Manchester United hierarchy are looking at, with Eddie Howe, Julian Nagelsmann and United States head coach Mauricio Pochettino the trio targeted.

#2070. Liverpool star’s agent confirms talks are underway over prospective new contract

The agent of one Liverpool player has confirmed that ‘preliminary talks’ are underway over a prospective new contract at Anfield for his client.

There has been much speculation in recent months regarding the long-term future of Alexis Mac Allister, who’s voiced his dismay at not being offered a new deal by the club earlier this year.

However, another South American stalwart in Alisson Becker revealed over the past couple of days that he’d be happy to remain on Merseyside beyond the expiry of his current terms next June.

The goalkeeper’s agent Ze Maria has indicated that negotiations over a contract renewal for the 34-year-old have commenced (albeit at an early stage) and that the Brazil international is very keen to remain with Liverpool.

Alisson’s representative told Egyptian news outlet WinWin: “Alisson loves Liverpool very much. We are in preliminary talks to renew the contract.”

(Photo by Justin Setterfield/Getty Images)

Following on from the Reds’ number 1 expressing his desire to extend his contract, this claim from his agent represents another positive sign that the veteran stopper will continue with LFC beyond next summer.

The Brazilian has been world-class throughout his eight-and-a-bit years at Anfield so far and, despite a couple of costly errors in recent games, was back to his exceptional best in the weekend victory over Crystal Palace as he pulled off a string of fantastic saves.

Liverpool tend not to hand out lucrative contract renewals to players in their mid-30s, but just as they made an exception for Mo Salah in April 2025, we wouldn’t rule out them doing the same for Alisson if he continues to exept.

The goalkeeper is currently on £250,000 per week, with only six of his teammates earning more at present (Capology), so it’d likely require another significant financial commitment to keep him at the club for another season or two.

It’ll likely take another few months before we know for certain whether or not he’ll still be a Reds player next term, but for now the indications are positive that we won’t have to say farewell to the legendary ‘keeper in six months’ time and can instead look forward to even more brilliance from him at Anfield after that.

#2069. Glaring summer 2026 Newcastle United transfer window blind spot now costing NUFC dear

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

The summer 2026 Newcastle United transfer window was incredibly busy.

A time of massive change at St James’ Park.

So many new Newcastle United signings coming in, even more players leaving.

The squad had a very different look once the Newcastle United transfer window closed in early September.

Fans left to evaluate the newcomers that we would be relying on to help navigate the 2026-27 season.

This is a breakdown of that Newcastle United transfer window when it comes to the signings made, the transfer fees are based on what was generally reported and they include the reported potential future add-ons in each case.

Goalkeepers £24.6m Ewen Jaouen, £25.7m Lukas Hornicek

Midfielders £23m Sean Steur, £35.5m Aladji Bamba, £52m Nico Gonzalez, £3m Ousmane Diabaté

Attacking players £43m Bazoumana Toure, £51m Matias Fernandez-Pardo

For me, there is a glaring summer 2026 Newcastle United transfer window blind spot that is now costing NUFC dear.

It feels like a lifetime ago now but let me take you back to the start of the 2025-26 Premier League season.

In the opening five Premier League matches of last season, Newcastle United kept four clean sheets.

In the opening 16 matches in all competitions of the 2025-26 season, Newcastle United kept seven clean sheets, The 16th game of last season was a 2-0 Champions League home win over Athletic Club.

As for the final 42 games of Newcastle United’s 2025-26 season, how many clean sheets were there?

The 4 January 2026 we had Newcastle 2 Crystal Palace 0, 18 January 2026 it was Wolves 0 Newcastle 0, 21 January 2026 we watched Newcastle 3 PSV 0, then 14 March 2026 we had Chelsea 0 Newcastle 1.

Only four clean sheets in the final 42 matches of Newcastle United’s 2025-26 season!

So for me, there is a glaring summer 2026 Newcastle United transfer window blind spot.

In such a busy summer window, there were nine signings made but only one defender.

Amar Dedic signed at full-back, but you also have to note that in that summer window, United got the likes of Kieran Trippier, Emil Krafth and Matt Targett off the books. Whilst Alex Murphy went out on loan for this season.

This 2026-27 season so far, Newcastle United have conceded 21 goals in 13 matches and still haven’t kept a clean sheet at home since January, ten months ago! If you don’t keep a clean sheet then you have to score at least two goals to stand any chance of winning.

Newcastle United don’t do grinding out 1-0 victories!

The last 1-0 United win at St James’ Park was against Wolves, Nick Woltemade’s scoring debut on 13 September 2025! Just the 14 months ago.

Away from home this season, Newcastle United have actually kept three clean sheets and all these matches ended unsurprisingly in victories. We have had 2-0 away wins at both Palace and Spurs in the league, plus a 1-0 at Millwall in the Carabao Cup.

On the Monday (10 March 2025) before the Carabao Cup final win over Liverpool, Newcastle won 1-0 away from home at West Ham in the Premier League thanks to a Bruno goal. That was exactly 20 months ago today, since then Newcastle have only had the three 1-0 victories, home or away (Wolves home in September 2025, away at Chelsea in March 2026, then Millwall away in September 2026).

We all know that the UEFA SCR impact meant Newcastle United had to sell some high transfer fee players and get a fair few high wage players off the books, whilst recruiting younger generally lower transfer fee and wage players to rebuild the squad.

However, it is baffling why more of the available resources wasn’t aimed at improving the defence. Especially when it is seen as far easier to find far better value in the overseas markets when recruiting promising defenders, than is is to land promising young players to score and create goals.

I am always generally wary of this modern trend of having a Sporting Director dictating which signings are targeted, rather than the manager being the main man.

For me the jury is very much out on Ross Wilson and the decisions he made this summer, both players sold and bought in.

The manager is surely far better placed to know the priorities in terms of positions that need strengthened and the players who can best fill them.

Wilson’s decision making means that when it comes to the back four, Newcastle United are still relying on the same four central defenders that were here last season when only four clean sheets in the final 42 games of the 2025-26 campaign.

Lewis Hall is the only left-back, whilst at right-back we now have Amar Dedic and the rarely fit enough to play Tino Livramento.

The ‘planning’ appears to be that if Dedic and Tino can also play left-back if needing to, whilst Lewis Miley can act as an emergency right-back. This can all swiftly fall apart though as we have already seen. With Tino unable to get and stay fully fit this season as he was injured in the summer, it means that Dedic gets injured and already we have Miley having to be right-back, which also then prevented him getting a run in the team where he needs to be in midfield AND where the team need him! Whilst we have been lucky that Lewis Hall is the team’s best player and hasn’t got injured, yet.

Meanwhile in the centre of defence, only 13 matches gone so far and a case of shuffling deckchairs around on the Titanic with no combination so far really convincing from the same quartet who were here last season! They include Schar and Burn who both turn 35 this season and surely at least one young promising central defender should have arrived in the summer window, both for this season AND the future.

#2067. Newcastle United consider January signing of Dion Drena Beljo after very positive scouting reports

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Dion Drena Beljo is a transfer target for Newcastle United.

The prolific Croatian striker under consideration for a January move to Tyneside.

Dion Drena Beljo has scored an astonishing 43 goals in 45 appearances for Dinamo Zagreb since joining them less than 16 months ago in July 2026.

He was only 21 when moving from club football to the Bundesliga in January 2023 and struggled with the step up in level as a young striker, only scoring five goals in 45 appearances for FC Augsburg.

After scoring 10 goals in 29 appearances for Rapid Wien in Austria, Dion Drena Beljo then getting the chance to return to Croatia and play for Dinamo Zagreb.

It has proved an inspired signing with those 43 goals in only 45 appearances.

On Sunday he scored in a 3-1 away league win over Varazdin, whilst last Thursday saw Dion Drena Beljo score as Dinano Zagreb outclassed Sunderland away from home in a 2-0 Europa League victory.

The i Paper say that their information is that Newcastle United have had Dion Drena Beljo watched across recent months and the scouting reports have fed back glowing reports.

The Dinamo Zagreb striker is now 24 and understood to be valued at around £20m.

The i Paper say that internally Newcastle United have been having intensive talks about adding a striker in the January transfer window as they look to support Matthias Jaissle after he walked into such a tough situation only 18 days before this 2026-27 season kicked off.

Newcastle United have scored only 13 goals in their 10 Premier League matches. Of their strikers, William Osula and Yoane Wissa have got one league goal each so far. The end of the summer transfer window saw the inexperienced 21-year-old Matias Fernandez-Pardo arrive, who can play as a number nine, but The i Paper report that as well as having injury issues after arriving ‘he didn’t look ready or equipped to play that role in his first four Premier League starts and might be better suited to playing in a wide role.’

The report says that the message coming out of the club is that the Newcastle United hierarchy are committed to Matthias Jaissle and that backing him with a new goalscorer in January would rubber stamp that backing. Whether that new striker/goalscorer ends up being Dion Drena Beljo remains to be seen.

Just over seven weeks to go before that January 2027 transfer window opens…

The Mag Podcast Episode 15: Fulham frustration, Newcastle United’s season report card and Relegation fears

Published: 9/11/2026 | Duration: 48 mins | Hosts: Jamie Smith, Simon Cahill & David Hutchinson

In Episode 15 of The Mag Pod, we find Jamie, Simon, and Hutch discussing their Fulham frustration. Relegation fears are debated and argued upon, with three months of the season having already passed by the trio give their Newcastle United season report card, so far.

You can play episode 15 on the audio player above or use your favourite podcast platforms including Spotify, Apple Podcasts, Amazon Music, YouTube, and Acast.

Listen to the latest episode of The Mag Pod! Join Jamie, Simon, and Hutch for unfiltered football debate.

Newcastle United’s frustrating draw at Fulham, a worrying lack of goals and an increasingly uncomfortable Premier League position dominate Episode 15 of The Mag Pod.

Jamie Smith is joined by Simon Cahill and David Hutchinson to assess the Magpies’ latest performance before delivering their quarter-season report cards, picking Newcastle’s standout players and signings, and debating whether this campaign could yet end in European qualification or a relegation battle.

The 1-1 draw at Craven Cottage provides plenty to discuss. Jacob Murphy’s spectacular strike earns praise, but Fulham’s equaliser, Harvey Barnes’ missed opportunities and Dan Burn’s controversially disallowed goal leave the lads wondering whether Newcastle have thrown away another valuable two points.

There’s particular concern about Newcastle’s attacking options. With goals proving difficult to come by, the panel questions whether the current forwards can provide what the team needs, while Hutch delivers a particularly unforgiving assessment of Harvey Barnes. Murphy’s celebration also comes under the microscope, prompting a wider discussion about supporters’ expectations and the criticism facing established members of the squad.

With ten Premier League matches played, it’s time for the first proper assessment of Newcastle United’s season. Jamie, Simon and Hutch reveal their grades, nominate their highlights, choose their best summer signings and identify their players of the season so far. Lewis Hall, Sean Steur and Lukas Horníček all feature in the conversation.

Looking ahead, the mood ranges from cautious optimism to outright pessimism. Could Newcastle recover sufficiently to challenge for European football, or is a relegation battle becoming a genuine possibility? And with Sunderland looming on the fixture list, there’s plenty of anxiety about what the coming months might bring.

There’s the usual humour, disagreements and increasingly desperate attempts to find positives, as the lads consider what supporters can realistically hope for from the rest of the season.

#2064. Claim that Bowen could jump ship in January ignores crucial point | West Ham News

As West Ham United slide out of the automatic promotion places – and indeed, after the Blackburn Rovers‘ result, out of the promotion play off spots too – so the Premier League vultures circle, reportedly, looking at key West Ham players who might be prepared to reconsider in January their own decisions to stay at London Stadium last summer.

Top amongst those is, of course, Jarrod Bowen. The Hammers’ skipper is still a full international and close to being the Irons’ record goal scorer in the Premier League, and it is well known he has signed a new contract with a paltry £15 million release clause allowing him to leave in the summer if promotion is not secured.

Goodisonnews.com. claim Everton’s struggles with Jack Grealish could lead under-pressure manager David Moyes to seek a reunion with Bowen in January:

“With Grealish on gigantic wages and not exactly given back the professionalism that deserves, could old interest in Bowen make perfect sense in January?

Bowen has signed on for another year come what may: He’ll be off next year of West Ham don’t get promoted, but not before

Captain of the Hammers, a more-recent England international, surely the threat of missing out on promotion could see the winger look at Everton more closely.”

If West Ham continue their slide, who could blame Everton for trying to prise Bowen away from London Stadium in the winter if the Hammers’ form continues to nosedive?

However I suspect anyone planning to grab Bowen in the winter underestimates his attachment to east London. Bowen has spoken many, many times of his love for the club and he is one of a dying breed for whom his word is his bond: Bowen has committed to seeing out the season as a Hammer, for better or for worse, and I’d see nothing, David Moyes’ personal invitation included, that would stop him fulfilling his side of the bargain.

Bless him. And build him a statue if he gets us promoted.
